# eqoa-rust-client-vanilla: an EQOA client in Rust, without mods

A native client for *EverQuest Online Adventures: Frontiers* that owns its own data
structures, rather than running a PS2 inside a program the way
eqoa-port (the decomp as a native program) does. The goal is every platform,
phones included (wgpu: Vulkan, Metal, DX12, WebGPU), 64-bit, with the feel of the
original matched exactly.

**No game data is in this repository**, and none is downloaded: you need your own
Frontiers disc image (natively the client asks for it the first time and keeps its
place, `discs.rs`; in a checkout `run/eqoa.iso`, or `$EQOA_ISO`). What the client reads out of
the game's code modules, CLIENT (the start menu, `gWorldInfo`, the race cameras) and
SUPPORT (the HUD's atlas, the UI sounds' volumes), it reads at run time, the way
eqoa-port's gendata does. The disc holds those modules only DNAS-encrypted, so crate
`dnas` unpacks them off it with the disc's own DNAS library, on threads of their own
while the session starts, and keeps them for the next run (`eqoa-rs/dnas` in the
platform's cache directory, or `$EQOA_CACHE`; in a page, the origin's storage). The
player's decrypted images serve instead when given (`run/client.bin` or
`$EQOA_CLIENT`, `run/support.bin` or `$EQOA_SUPPORT`).

## Vanilla

The game as the PS2 plays it, with the client's own additions for a PC, a phone
or a browser (each described where it lives below):

- **Widescreen**: the HUD at a wide window's edges; the start menu, its
  windows and the merchant's window the game's 4:3 picture in the middle,
  closed at its edges.
- **A frame rate untethered** from the game's 30 frames a second (the game's
  logic still steps at 30).
- **The mouse and the touch screen.**
- **The keyboard**, with WASD walking, and the options' **Controls** page: every
  key and button can be moved, and a controller's button can take any of the
  keyboard's own actions (the map on R3, Hide UI, F9's art).
- **Macros, as PCSX2's** (the user's of 2026-11-06, "pcsx2 has a nice macro system", and of
  2026-11-10, "it is not the same yet. lets also add it as its own menu entry": `macros`, `macropage`,
  `binds::Macro`, read off PCSX2's `pcsx2/SIO/Pad/Pad.cpp` and its editors): the options' own **Macros**
  page, beside Controls, sixteen macros listed on its left as PCSX2's editor lists them. Each has
  triggers (up to four, any one of which runs it), each a chord of up to four keys, controller buttons
  and sticks pushed (or L2 and R2 pulled) held together, bound as PCSX2 binds one (held, kept as one is
  let go, five seconds to give up); what it presses, all at once, of the game's sixteen buttons and the
  sticks' eight ways; Press To Toggle; a Frequency (0 held, N let go and pressed again every N fields,
  60 a second, two to each of the game's frames, at which the macros act, so the game sees what a
  PS2's would at any display rate: 2 is 15 presses a second, 4 is 7.5, 1 held or nothing by the
  trigger's half of the frame); a Pressure (how far it pushes a stick: the game reads every button as
  pressed or not; under 24% at rest, a walk from 24%, a run from 60%); a Deadzone (how far a stick or a
  trigger's pull in a trigger must go). The pad is set as PCSX2's is, the last change of each button
  and stick's way having it, a controller's or a macro's. An input stays in one place: a trigger's key
  is no row's (a row's key is refused), its button leaves the game's field and its stick's way the
  game's stick, until a row or field takes it back (and with it every trigger it was in). Kept in
  `keys.opt` (`macro.N.trigger=key:y & pad:l1`, `.buttons`, `.toggle`, `.frequency`, `.pressure`,
  `.deadzone`; the build of 2026-11-06's `.key`, `.button` and `.turbo` read into them). Nothing new
  goes to a server: the game sees a pad's presses.
- **Copy and paste in the text boxes** (the user's of 2026-11-07, `clipboard`, `editbox`): Ctrl+V,
  Ctrl+C, Ctrl+X (⌘ on a Mac), natively and in a page (not on Android, whose keyboard pastes as
  typing). A paste takes only what a console's keyboard can type, because the line reaches consoles:
  a PS2's text box writes past its stack on a run of `|x` (`editbox::typable`). Line ends stay only
  in the two boxes where a console's Enter types them (a mail's body, a petition).
- **Chat lines to the clipboard** (the user's of 2026-11-07, `chat`): with a mouse, natively and in a
  page (not on a phone), a drag over the chat box picks whole lines, on past its edges through the
  1,000 kept, and the Copy key takes them as plain text, a line a row, the game's `|` codes out; with
  none picked and no edit box up, the newest line. Esc or a click elsewhere clears them. The game's
  chat is drawn as it was, the highlight over it; a click on a line is still the chat line, and a drag
  anywhere else still the camera.
- **A GM's flight** (the user's of 2026-11-07, `walker`, `Session::flying`): on a server that turns it on
  (go-eqoa-server's `#fly`, opcode 0x7E1B), the game's swim path with no gravity, no surface and a land
  body's gaits and flags, going where the camera looks at the walk's own speed: an orbit's default tilt (the follow
  orbit's 15 degrees, the orbit's 25) counts as level, and first person and the follow camera, which have none, go
  by their own pitch. The console frames are kept as ever; `vi` is untouched. Nothing for anyone a server does not
  turn it on for.
- **A jump** (the user's of 2026-11-09, "lets add jump to the game"; `walker`, `jump`, `Session::jumps`): on a server
  that offers it (go-eqoa-server's `world.jump`, opcode 0x7E28, offered only to a client that answered its bug report
  offer, so never to a PS2), Space (V too; a Controls row; a controller has none until one is given it there: every
  button of the pad is the game's) jumps 0.9 high, half the walk's body: the game's own walk given a start speed up (7.1 a second) and
  the floor it remembers lifted over the jump's top, so its own gravity, air (nothing pushes in the air, the run is
  carried across, a running jump goes 5 ahead) and landing take it, with the fall's clip in the air and the landing's
  after, 0.6 s on flat ground. Not in a liquid, in the air, flying, casting, held (0x00FC or no speed) or with a window
  up, and only once a report standing has gone out since the last landing or move (the server judges a jump from one).
  Others on this client see a player in the air at the height the server sends (`jump::aloft`: CLIENT stands every
  creature on the floor within 1.5 of it, so a PS2 shows a jumper on the ground playing the fall), its fall wholly in
  by the top of its arc as drawn and its landing played once it comes down (`jump::Air`; the user's of the same day,
  "the jump needs a falling animation once you hit the zenith": its first update in the air comes 0.1-0.3 s into the
  jump, and the fall's 250 ms blend from there was half in at the top). The console frames are kept as ever, and a
  client that may jump and does not walks bit for bit as one that may not; `vi` is untouched. Space was the attack's
  until then: the attack is on G, and Space, while the jump's row has it, is still the attack's wherever it makes no
  jump (a server without jumps, a window up: `binds::KeyEdge::space_attacks`).
  Nothing on a server that does not offer it.
- **1,000 lines of chat history** (the game's ring holds 31).
- **The start menu's windows over the world**, with no green backdrop behind
  them.
- **The Extended options**, a page for each of Video (Display: a window or full
  screen, as F11 and Alt+Enter toggle it; Brightness, Frame Rate, VSync, Render
  Scale: x1, x2 or x4, Loading Screens: Longer or Instant), World (Fog, Mipmaps,
  Flora, Environment, Ground Fog: PS2 or Exact, Art), Lighting (Dynamic Lighting, Sun Shadows, Light Shadows,
  Shadow Distance: 250 to 1000, Ambient Occlusion, Particles: PS2 or Exact), UI (Ability Bars, Update
  Notices, Text: Sharp or Smooth, HUD: Nearest or Sharp, Raid List, Pet Effects), Controller (Controller
  Focus, Capture Controller, and the touch screen's pad: Touch Stick Size and Touch Button Size,
  50 to 200% of the pad as it was, larger only as far as the screen has room; Touch Opacity, 10
  to 200%; Touch Edge, 0 to 10% of the screen in from its sides), Camera (Distance, Turn Speed, Screen Shake),
  Characters (Level of Detail, NPC/PC Display and Distance, Name Distance,
  Overlapping Names, Shadows), Sound (Sound in Background, Output Device: the system's
  default, followed when it changes, or a device by its name), natively Textures
  (Texture Packs, Dump Textures, and on a computer Texture Folder), Map (Map,
  Minimap, Map Markers, Quest Helper, Quest Tracker) and Number Colors (Your Damage,
  Your Procs, Your Heals, Pet Damage, Pet Procs, Pet Heals), and the rows below.
- **The map and the quest helper** (the user's of 2026-11-09: "also add the map and
  quest helper we had in a former update"; they were taken out on 2026-10-27 with the
  others below that are not the game's, and only these two came back): Extended > Map,
  each row on by default. The Map row off takes away the map, the minimap, the
  waypoints on the compass and the group's waypoint, and the Quest Helper row the
  quests' marks, the tracker and the guide; with both off the game is as it is without
  them (M, N and the compass do nothing, a group line is a chat line, no server is
  asked for marks or a tracker and nothing is built or kept).
- **The map** (M), drawn from the player's own disc for every world, on any
  server, with a waypoint of the player's own (a click or a tap on the map,
  □ or Q at its middle once the map is moved, on the target while it follows
  the player, a white ring marking the target; shown on the compass's rim,
  kept per world, sent nowhere), **a group waypoint** (□ or Q held, O, Shift
  and a click, a finger held on the map; at □'s spot, else where the player
  stands) said in the group's own chat as "Waypoint in Tunaria at x 24611,
  z 15502 near Freeport", one every five seconds, which every vanilla client
  in the group with the Map on puts on its map, minimap and compass in orange
  with its sender's name (only that wording, on the group's channel, from a member of
  the group; a PS2 player reads the line; gone when reached, when its sender
  or the player leaves the group, or with a click on it), and **the map's XP
  camps and coaches**: EQOA-Data's XP camps and
  every coach marked, each with a card of what it is (a camp's levels, the
  guide's note, the rare creatures near it and notable drops; a coach's town,
  coachman and route), the coach routes as lines and the towns' names (X or
  ○ hides the markers, as Extended's Map Markers does, T or Select the names).
- **Community markers on the map**: places players suggested and a GM
  accepted, one list for every player of this client on any server, a pin
  each (its colour its kind: a landmark, a camp, a quest's place, a merchant,
  a way to travel, a danger) with its name in close and a card under the
  pointer, shown and hidden with the markers (X, ○). The list is kept on
  eqoaclassic.com and asked there when the map opens, and every ten minutes
  while it is up, whatever game server is played on: a request to our
  server, on Sandstorm too (the last list is kept, for a start offline). N
  (a controller's L3, the map's Suggest button) suggests one, the spot the
  player chose last: a waypoint put since the map opened, else the place
  under the pointer (a controller's middle cross once the map is moved), else
  the target's (its name typed in already), else an older waypoint, else
  where the player stands, with a name and a note typed in the game's edit
  box; it goes to eqoaclassic for
  a GM's approval, as the spell visuals' suggestions do, and nothing is sent
  without the player's Enter on a name.
- **Quest marks** on the map and the minimap (the Quest Helper's), from a server
  that sends them (go-eqoa-server does, 0x7E03/0x7E04), else the client's own
  quest guide's (below; Sandstorm sends none): a gold "!" at each NPC with a
  quest the player may take now (its class, race and level), a "?" where a
  step of a quest under way is to be done (an NPC to see, or the nearest
  places of the creatures a kill or loot step hunts), over the camps and
  coaches, each with a card of its quests as theirs have (their names and
  levels, the start of the offer or the step's journal line, and the rewards
  or what the step needs) under the mouse, on a tap (a second puts the
  waypoint on it, as a click does), or under the pad's middle cross; on the
  minimap too (a "?" on its rim while farther than it shows, a "!" only where
  it lies on its pictures: 2026-11-09, the owner's "they are everywhere", a
  level 60's dozens rang the rim; the tooltip under the mouse); a "?" drawn
  over a "!" beside it; J or R3 (or the map's Quests button) hides them, kept.
- **A GM travels by the map**: a server that offers it (go-eqoa-server, to a
  GM's client alone with the GM's channel: 0x7E34, `Session::map_travel`) has
  the map take Ctrl and a click (⌘, or the Super key, as well) for a trip to
  the spot under it (a quest mark's, a marker's, else the ground's): Play asks
  for it (0x7E35: u32 world, f32 x, f32 z; `Session::map_travel_to`), the
  server checks the GM's level and puts the character on the ground's floor
  there, and the map closes; the legend says "Ctrl+click Travel here" ("Cmd+"
  on a Mac) only while it is offered. Anyone else's Ctrl and a click is a
  click. A playshot's `EQOA_MOUSE` takes `ctrl down|up@<ms>`.
- **The quest tracker** (the Quest Helper's, and its own Quest Tracker row),
  from a server that sends it (go-eqoa-server does, 0x7E05/0x7E06), else the
  quest guide's: the quests under way under the minimap and above the chat box, with no backing,
  the words outlined, each quest's name in gold and what its step asks
  counted under it ("Bat Eye: 0/3", "Speak with Kellina"), one counted whole
  dimmed; as many as fit, then "+N more"; a click on it opens the quest
  journal (closed, back to the world); hidden with the minimap (Hide UI, a
  window open, the map up). A line too wide wraps onto a second row.
- **The quest guide** (`questguide`, the Quest Helper's; the user: "let it be built
  into client also, so sandstorm can benefit without server additions, though may not be as
  accurate"): on a server that offers no quest marks or tracker of its own in
  the session (3 s into the world), the same marks and tracker made by the
  client, every card and tracked quest saying "(guide)", from a table compiled
  in (`tools/gen_quest_guide.py`: EQOA-Data's 746 class quests of levels 1-20,
  MIT, as go-eqoa-server's quest files hold them, and where their people stand
  on go-eqoa-server). A journal line is a quest's step when it names its NPC
  or items, among the quests of the player's class, race, level and home city
  (Sandstorm's own wording: "Return to Nothard.", "Bring Norgram 2 slashed orc
  pawn belts ."); a line naming nothing it knows shows as its text. A "!" is
  for the next quest of the home city's chain (its race and class's start, or
  the city its quests named), or of a chain begun elsewhere. Items are
  counted from the inventory, worn ones too; a kill step's kills as the client
  saw them by the player's or its pet's hand, marked "(seen)". What it keeps
  of a character (`questguide.opt`: the quests it finished, its home city, the
  kills seen) never leaves the computer.
- **The minimap** (Extended > Map > Minimap, on unless turned off): the map's pictures round
  the player under the quick menus' name plate, left of their icons (out at a wide
  window's right and up a tall one's top with them), north up, with the group, the
  creatures and players in view, the target, the waypoint (on its rim while
  farther) and marks of other kinds by `map::mini::Mark` (camps, coach stops,
  quests: their tooltips under the mouse); the wheel over it zooms, a click or a
  tap opens the map; shown where the quick menus are, never under Hide UI. N
  shows or hides it, kept as the Minimap row is.
- **Dynamic Lighting** (the user's of 2026-11-08, phase 1; Extended > Lighting, off
  by default, and off the PS2's light exactly): the strip programs' light worked out
  for every pixel instead of every vertex (the sun and the personal light, the same
  colours, ranges and falloff), and up to 16 point lights more, which the console's
  raster (one point light, the personal light's) never draws: the fires the zones place
  (each additive emitter's flame: torches, braziers, candles, street lamps' halos), the
  zones' placed point lights (none on the disc's worlds), and the spells', glowing
  weapons' and fiery creatures' particles and the spells' bolts while they play; chosen
  as the engine's own emitter query would choose them (`lights.rs`, `render::lit`). A
  baked actor is not lit by the fires again: its colour buffers carry them. A spell's
  light is held from frame to frame (`lights::Moment`, petition 1321): it swells in and
  fades out over about a third of a second and never flashes more than three times a
  second, the limit photosensitivity guidance (WCAG 2.2) sets, where each frame's own,
  made of the few particles drawn then, jumped up to 18 times a second; a light that
  another takes the place of among the 16 dims out, and the other in, over a quarter of
  a second (`lights::Chooser`).
- **Ambient Occlusion** (the user's of 2026-11-08, "lets add ambient occlusion";
  Extended > Lighting, off by default, and off the game's picture exactly): the opaque
  scene's creases, corners and contacts darkened, every pixel by how much of the space
  round it is filled, as the light that fills a scene reaches less of them: a wall's foot,
  a room's corners, under ledges and eaves, round what stands on the ground and under
  everyone's feet (`render::ao`, from the frame's depth). Water, glows, the effect
  volumes' fog, the particles and spells, the blob shadows, the names and the HUD draw over
  it as they were, and it fades out with distance and into the fog.
- **Sun Shadows** (2026-11-08; Extended > Lighting, off by default, drawn only with
  Dynamic Lighting on, and off every frame as it was): the sun's light stopped by
  what stands between a surface and the sun, which the strip programs let through
  everything. Walls, roofs, trees (their leaves cut out), rocks, hills and the
  characters cast shadows that move with the time of day, a little every frame,
  out to 250 before the camera; none at night, when the game's "sun" is under the
  horizon. The light only ever goes where the console's goes: a surface's sun is
  the lesser of its bake's and the shadow's, so what a bake darkened is not
  darkened twice (`render::sunshadow`).
- **Light Shadows** (2026-11-09, the user's "sun shadows lets make all light sources
  act similar"; Extended > Lighting, off by default, drawn only with Dynamic Lighting
  on, and off every frame as it was): the point lights' light stopped as the sun's is,
  the fires' (torches, braziers, candles, lamps), the personal light's and the
  spells'. A cube map round each of the frame's most important lights (by brightness
  times reach over distance: 8 natively, 4 in a page and on Android), what does not
  move drawn once into a cache of it once the light stands still and the characters
  and props over a copy of it each frame; a light that moves (the personal light as
  the player walks, a spell's) drawn whole each frame. When the budget hands a map on,
  the leaving light's shadow fades out and the coming one's in, a quarter of a second
  each. The personal light's own body casts nothing for it (a skinned body whose box's
  footprint holds the lantern, and a cape; not a held weapon, whose box straddled that
  line in an idle stance and cast nothing standing still), nor a body round a light,
  nor a placed thing no more than 6 across whose box holds it (a street lamp's
  housing round its light, whose floor darkened a square under every lamp); nothing
  nearer a light than 0.5 does; and a light of the moment (a held item's glow, a
  spell's), which rides the body or weapon carrying it, casts what does not move alone
  (its carrier's shadow flickered as it went in and out of the carrier's box), from
  where its map was drawn while it sways within 15 cm of it (an idle weapon's glow
  crawled every edge it cast). Each shadow's edge is a tent of the map's texels three
  each way (two in a page and on Android), soft and gliding as a light or a caster
  moves: four comparisons half a texel apart before it let each texel that flipped
  under a moving light move the shadow up to half its depth at once, every edge crawling
  a texel at a time and showing the texels' steps up close (the owner's "blocky and
  also jittery", 2026-11-10) (`render::lightshadow`).
  **Shadow Distance** (the user's "the light shadows need a slider to raise to a
  greater distance"; a slider, the game's 250 to 1000 in 125s): the sun's far two
  cascades stretched (the third by the scale's square root, the last to the distance,
  the near two kept) and the lights shadowed raised evenly from 8 to all 16 Dynamic
  Lighting draws (4 to 8 in a page and on Android); at 250 every number as before.
- **The raid list** (the user's of 2026-11-07, "show names and hp in a way to
  accomodate"; `raidlist.rs`): on a server that has raid groups (go-eqoa-server: a
  group past four, its 0x7E18-0x7E1A), every member's name over a thin health bar in
  place of the group's bars, which hold three, in up to four columns and past them
  by the wheel. A click targets a member the client can see, and L1's cycle reaches
  them. Extended > UI's Raid List, on by default; a client that does not ask gets the
  game's bars, as a PS2 does.
- **The pet's effects under its bar** (the server owner's of 2026-11-09, "jibcro is
  showing pets buffs as if on him, put the buff icons under the pet name plate";
  `hud::pet_effects_pieces`): CLIENT shows a pet's effects nowhere, so on a server
  that sends them (go-eqoa-server's 0x7E32/0x7E33: each effect's icon, time left and
  name, whenever they change) a row of their icons goes under the pet's bar, the
  player's own effects' strip at half its size, each gone as it runs out; a click on
  it targets the pet. The player's own effects window (channel 0x43) stays the
  player's own, on every client. Extended > UI's Pet Effects, on by default; off, or
  on a server that sends none, nothing is drawn, as on a PS2.
- **Number colours by what made them** (the server owner's of 2026-11-09, of a player's
  ask "to see/change colors of damage/procs/healing for player/pets similar to the way SS
  does": "we can add the number colors to extended menu"; `numcolor.rs`): CLIENT colours
  a floating number by its sign and by who made it, and a pet's hit reaches its owner as
  the owner's own, so on a server that says what made each (go-eqoa-server's tags: 0x7E38
  offers, 0x7E39 answers 1 while a row is not Default, then one byte after 0x00DB's body,
  `net::session::number_tag`) the player's own figures and its pet's are drawn in the
  colour chosen for their row: Your Damage (a swing, a spell or ability, a damage over
  time's tick, a damage shield), Your Procs, Your Heals (and a heal over time's tick), and
  the pet's three. Extended > Number Colors, each row Default (the game's own colour) or one
  of `numcolor::PALETTE`, every row Default by default; an untagged number, a number taken,
  a heal received and a server's patch's own colours (Sandstorm's S2) are the game's as ever.
- **A render scale and a sharp HUD**: Render Scale (off unless chosen) x2 or
  x4 draws the whole picture, the world and everything over it, two or
  four times the window's size each way and averages it down, which smooths
  every edge, leaf and fence and changes nothing else (the game's texture
  levels go by distance, not by pixels), at the most the graphics card allows
  where the window is too large (Video's page says so); HUD Sharp (off unless
  chosen: the default is Nearest, the game's sampling, as Sharp costs more to
  draw: the user, 2026-10-28) draws the windows' and the HUD's art with every
  texel as wide as every other at any window size (Nearest makes some a pixel
  wider than others). The world's textures stay as
  the game filters them: a crisp filter for them was tried and taken out the
  same day (the user: "world textures cant render proper without smooth. so
  only use for UI, Text").
- **More ability bars**: the Extended page's Ability Bars adds a third and a
  fourth, five abilities each, on the HUD and in the spell book, on a server
  that keeps abilities past the game's ten slots.
- **SOE's final toolbars**, on a server that keeps weapon sets (go-eqoa-server
  does): the item quick slots take two potions and two gems, and a fourth HUD
  menu holds four named weapon sets and bare hands, a set made by Prepare on a
  weapon and taken up from the HUD, as the live game had them at its end.
- **SOE's final mail**, on a server that offers it (go-eqoa-server's 0x7E0A;
  Sandstorm and eqoa.live keep the game's): a gram to several names, the ones
  unknown named in the box and the ones not delivered to named in red with
  why, the To line in the reader and its Reply All. On every server, as the
  live game had them: one Master Class at a time (a second refused in red),
  the destroy and sell boxes naming the item (selling loot still asks
  nothing), a closed petition's -8 saying it has been addressed, the inbox's
  rows going round, and the GMs' /rst_mastr, /rst_both, /rem_abil,
  /banktunar and /servdown. The live game's words they need are
  `livewords.rs`'s, put into every WORDS.TXT that lacks them.
- **SOE's final group search and auction stacks**, on a server that sends the
  live game's feature word (go-eqoa-server does; Sandstorm does not, and keeps
  the disc's): the Group menu's Search, Register and Unregister and `/lfg`,
  the window that lists the players looking for a group by race, class and
  level; a stack put up at the auction by the item, a bid for some of a lot or
  all of it, as the live game had them at its end.
- **Duels as the live game had them**, on a server that has them (its
  feature word, 0x07F5, with the duel bit: go-eqoa-server's; Sandstorm and
  eqoa.live have none): Community > Duel > Invite and Surrender, the live
  client's duel window (`VIWndDuel`: the invitation with its countdown, and
  the wager, the trade window's twin, tunar staked), and the opponent and its
  pet in reach as the live `TargetInRange` and `CycleTarget` allow (the user,
  2026-10-29: "port the duel window to vanilla too").
- **A server's own race by its own name**, on a server that has them
  (go-eqoa-server's custom races, each passing as a race of the game's on the
  wire; `racelabel.rs`): where the game shows a named character's race, the
  server's word for it (the user, 2026-11-07: "my race says ogre, but lets make
  it Hill Giant, Titan as MC"). Its camera and what it may wear stay the race it
  passes as.
- **Armour on bodies made without it** (the user's of 2026-11-08, "Lay armour textures on", a
  prototype; `slotless`): a body with no texture slots (the kappa, brownie, pixie, fairy, minotaur,
  dryad, skeleton, fungusman, gargoyle and hill giant a server's races wear) shows the armour, robe and
  dye in its look, laid over its torso, arms, hands, legs and feet as they sit on a human: its triangles
  are carried onto HumanMale (HumanFemale for a female body) bone by bone, and take the slot and texture
  coordinates of what a human shows there in the same look. Heads, necks, wings and tails keep their
  own look, and so do the parts of a body's own model the user named (`slotless::KEPT`: the
  fungusman's bag and strap, and its fungus, "the fungus that protrudes"); a triangle skinned to the
  spine that runs along an arm to the elbow is the arm's (the fungusman's upper arms, which have no
  vertex of their own, take the human's sleeve). The skeleton, whose bones are too thin to carry
  armour, wears a human's armour itself (the user: "fit armor on skeletons like humans ... dont make
  the armor model formfitting"): it is built on HumanMale's skeleton, so HumanMale's own surfaces for
  each worn slot go on its bones, drawn from inside too (a hollow shell round thin bones: culled as a
  human's skin is, its far side was seen through), and its bones under them are hidden; where nothing
  is worn they stay bare. Its helm goes on HumanMale's head node where its body names none (the 2002
  disc's skeleton, on the 2002 HumanMale's skeleton: `slotless::helm_node`), and sits so the skull
  stays inside it (the owner, 2026-11-09: "the skeleton helm has its head clipping out the back
  slightly"; `slotless::helm_fit`): that human's node carries none of the stretch SOE gave Frontiers'
  skeleton's own helm node (a FixScale of 1.1 across and front to back), so the back of the skull came
  out through every 2002 helm by 4 to 13.5 mm. Each skeleton a player may wear has its helm stretched
  about its node and moved front to back by a table measured off the meshes (`slotless::SEATS`, printed
  by `tests::measure_helm_fits`): the least change that holds every vertex of the skull half a
  millimetre inside each of the eight helms, in every pose (the skull rides the helm's node rigidly in
  every action); Frontiers' own skeleton needs none, the three its options offer (skulls a tenth larger)
  a few millimetres for their cheeks. The great skeletons a Skeleton player may choose are built on the
  troll's skeleton (its 43 nodes, every bind pose but the skull's top) and wear the troll's armour so
  (`slotless::SHELL_BODIES`); their helms, on their own node in Frontiers (SOE's FixScale 1.4, 1.2, 1.2)
  and on the 2002 HumanMale's in the 2002 art (whose skeleton the 2002 disc builds them on), take a few
  millimetres for their cheeks too (`SEATS`). A body whose texture slots name some of the five worn
  slots and not the others (2026-11-10, the owner: "some of the new playable races variant models dont
  support visual equipment yet": 79 of the 148 bodies the server's races may wear, every creature's with
  slots of its own, the froglok's, kerran's, aviak's, lizardman's, gnoll's, orc's, kobold's, ratman's and
  satyr's: a chest, bracers and legs, no gloves or boots) keeps its own slots as a PS2 dresses them and
  gains the ones it lacks as a slotless body does, over the triangles its own slots' entries do not draw
  (`slotless::lacking`, `bare_part`); a body with all five (every player race's) is dressed exactly as
  before (held to every CSprite on the disc). A NodeIDList naming one foot for both footstep slots (the
  ratman trickster's) is read with the other foot the node mirrored across the root.
  **Helms** on those bodies (the owner, the same day: "the helmets are not showing for many races"): no
  creature's body names a helm node in Frontiers (the 2002 disc's goblins and some of its orcs do, SOE's
  own seat: their node of the human's helm node's name, scaled 0.9 and 1.19), so a player's helm hung
  nowhere on 136 of them. Each family's bodies now hang it on that named node at the top of the head, or,
  where it is low on the head (the lizardman's skeleton, the gnolls' and minotaurs' too, the kobold's and
  the ratman's) or missing (the froglok's, kerran's, aviak's, kappa's, pixie's, fairy's and satyr's), on the
  head node moved to the top of it, stretched to the head (`slotless::HEAD_SEATS`, judged by eye: the head
  inside the helm as a human's is, a snout, beak or muzzle through the face, ears, horns and crests through
  the top). The fungusmen wear none (`HEADLESS`: their cap is their head; a helm hides under it or swallows
  the body). A body naming a helm node of its own (the fire-weaver orc's) keeps it; NPCs keep none.
  Players' characters only (a creature whose name colour is a player's, 2, and the pregame's figures):
  NPCs keep the look the PS2 gave them (the user: "Players only").
  `eqoa dress <body> <out.png> [set=N robe=R dye=RRGGBB ...]` draws a body in a look from four sides;
  `helm=N` with its helm (`helmfit=0` as the node alone seats it), `era=2002` in the 2002 disc's art,
  `frame=head` framed on the head, `helmseat=A,s,ox,oy,oz` (or `A,sx,sy,sz,ox,oy,oz`; `A` `named`,
  `head` or a node's number) a helm seat tried in place of the body's.
- **Weapons on a server's race whose body holds none** (petition 1278, a Gargoyle: "gargoyles dont show
  equiped weapons"; `racebody`): a CSprite whose header says `npc` 0 holds no items on a PS2 (`AttachItem`
  returns at once while `m_flags & 1` is clear), and the gargoyles' body (0xB4D2D132) is one, the only one
  of the bodies go-eqoa-server's races wear. A player's character drawn in a body one of the server's races
  wears (creation's list, 0x7E1F) holds its items as a race's body does: on the nodes its ASlotList names (the
  gargoyle's two hands), its shield, where the body names no node for one, on the off hand's hand; its hands
  take their weapons' types and projectiles (its swings stay its own two claw attacks). NPCs, forms and
  illusions keep the PS2's look, and every body that held items before holds them on the same nodes (held to
  every CSprite on the disc). The fairy's shield node (16), which its animations leave at its art's size while
  they draw both its hands' at 0.3 (the pixie's draw all three so), is given a FixScale of 0.3 for such a player
  (`racebody::SHIELD_FIX`): its shield was three and a third times the fairy's size. `eqoa dress <body> <out.png>
  main=ID off=ID shield=ID [race=1] [action=N]` draws what it holds.
- **Emotes on a server's own races** (the user's of 2026-11-08, "lets add emotes to the new playable races";
  `raceemote`): a wave, bow, point or cheer is an action every client looks up in the body's own play list
  (`PlayEmote` on the player's sprite, 0x0775 on another's), and eighteen of the bodies go-eqoa-server's races
  wear have none (the brownie's and the skeleton's have the human's own): `SetDefaultEntries` gives them the
  idle, so such a player's emote was its idle starting over, as it still is on a PS2. A player's character in
  one of them, on a server with races of its own, plays what a small table chosen by sight says
  (`raceemote::TABLE`): the human's emote carried onto the body's skeleton where it is built on the human's bone
  names (the body's own bone lengths, its tail and wings at rest), else its own nearest (the pixie's, fairy's
  and fungusman's). Another body a race's options offer a player (the ice giant's, a goblin king's, the faun's)
  plays its race's own body's (`raceemote::poses_of`: the body row's first step). The emote's entry itself is replaced, so a player's own emote and another player's are the
  same; NPCs and every other body keep the game's (held to every CSprite on the disc). `eqoa emotes <body>
  <out.png> [wave bow point cheer N human:N ...]` draws a body's emotes, or its actions, a row each. The fall
  and the landing the same way (2026-11-09, "Yes, borrow the human's"; `raceemote::grant_air`): of the table's
  bodies only the brownie's, dryad's, orc's and skeleton's have their own (every player race's body has), the
  rest stood in the air; on a server with jumps a player's character in one of them takes the human's fall
  (looping) and landing (once) carried onto it, or its own nearest (`AIR_OWN`: the pixie's, fairy's and
  fungusman's), in its own view and every other player's, a jump and a fall off a ledge alike.
- **The Wisp** (a race of go-eqoa-server's own, 31; the owner, 2026-11-09: "the armor will form like a human and also
  the weapons swing like humans. like when humans are invis, but the wisp is inside the helm"; `wisp`): a human's body
  of its sex, marked by a race option the server lays always (a texture row of one step, `wisp`, whose value is the
  will o' wisp's body, CHAR.ESF 0x8143DC70, no texture: an older client lays nothing). Drawn as its worn slots' triangles
  alone (where `ApplyLook` lays armour or a robe; no skin, face or hair), the wisp a head across on the helm's node
  inside any helm, hung with the held items and the helm (`play::attach`): the player's own, others', character select
  and the create pages. A PS2 shows a human in its armour. **The Jal'Raeth** (30): their three bodies wear a helm on
  the head node they name (`slotless::HEAD_HELMS`: they name no helm node), the human's chest, bracers and gloves
  carried onto their skeleton by bone names (`slotless::named_shell_of`: their hands and feet hang off the root, so
  the fit by limbs finds none), and the human's emotes on their upper body (`raceemote::TABLE`).
- **Capes**, on a server that has them (go-eqoa-server's `world.capes`, the Heirloom Test
  Server's; the server owner, 2026-11-08: "lets add a new equip slot for heirloom server only.
  capes for all. there are many capes we can borrow from for various styles."; `cape`): the
  server offers them after the first character list (0x7E25) and this client answers at once;
  from then on a cape item comes as wear slot 28, worn in equip slot 22 (no retail table has
  either: a PS2, and this client on any other server, gets the item as a plain one and never
  the slot), the list's characters' capes by server id (0x7E27) and the capes of the players
  seen by entity id (0x7E26), each the whole set. The item menu equips one as any wearable
  (`inventory::can_wear_in`, `infotext`: Slot: Cape). The art is the disc's own capes
  (`cape::STYLES`, by the style the server's item names): the silver knight's (0xD1300BD3),
  Magnus Jaeger's (0xCE1B8083), the Dawn Maiden's (0xD7540FDA) and Dread's (0xDC3B3450),
  alone or with its fur mantle, each its body's cape materials cut out, and drawn in the
  item's colour when it has one. Every player race's body is built on one of two skeletons
  (forty nodes or forty-three, the same bone for bone across the races); a cape goes on the
  one it was made on as it is, on the other bone for bone by role, and on a server's races'
  bodies by their parts (spine by height, head, arms from the hands, legs from the thighs), so
  a cape whose corners ride the arms folds with them; wider and further back on a broader
  body. A goblin (race 13's own body and every one its options offer, `cape::CROUCHED`; the
  owner, 2026-11-10: "on goblins they are too big") is bound standing and rests in a crouch,
  its shoulders at 0.70 of their bind height, so a cape sized on its bind pose hung to the
  ground: its cape is sized as it stands, its height by that share (`cape::standing`), every
  other body's exactly as before. It names the wearer's nodes in its bind pose and is drawn on the body's own palette,
  swaying with the robe-tail bones every player skeleton's walk swings. Players' characters
  only, in the world, on the player and at character select (the bodies a list's capes are
  cut from read out of CHAR.ESF there, `res::Res::want_bodies`); bodies with wings (the
  pixie's, the fairy's, the gargoyle's) wear none. With none worn nothing is made or drawn.
  `eqoa dress <body> <out.png> cape=N [capecolor=RRGGBB]` draws one; `EQOA_CAPE_DEBUG=1`
  says how a cape was fitted.
- **A pack and a bank past CLIENT's 40**, on a server that has them (go-eqoa-server's `world.pack_slots`,
  `world.bank_slots`, the Heirloom Test Server's 100 each; the server owner, 2026-11-09: "lets increase inventory and
  bank space to 100"; `net::session::OP_ITEM_LISTS`, `inventory::pack_cap`, `bank_cap`): CLIENT's `VIItemList` is 40
  records and no more (`ItemInternal[40]`, `AddItem(.., 40)` everywhere; `Read` takes a dump's count with no check),
  so the server offers more after the first character list (0x7E29: 1, the pack's, the bank's) and this client answers
  at once with what it holds of each (the offer's, at most 255: the server names a record by a byte); before every
  MemoryDump the server says the sizes in force (0x7E2A), which the lists fill to (`add_item`'s capacity at every
  site: loot, a purchase, a trade's copy, a catch, a craft's copy, the bank's 0x1251, the stat preview) and every
  window shows: the item menu's four tabs (the definitions' 40 each, or the pack's), the bank's two lists, the
  merchant's selling list (its wares stay a merchant's 40), the trade's, the corpse window's, the forge's, the
  auction's bank list (its `Init(80)`, or the bank's) and the experiment's components, each scrolling as the game's
  lists do. Worn items count among the pack's, as among CLIENT's 40. A server that never says, a shard switched to,
  and a PS2 keep 40 of each; the server sends a PS2 the first 40 (worn first) and keeps the rest for the character.
- **A server's race's own customisation**, on a server that sends it (the user, 2026-11-08: "lets add char
  customization to the new races, if any other faces and hair add them, but if not add color swaps";
  go-eqoa-server's offer, 0x7E22, the options, 0x7E23, and the looks chosen, 0x7E24; `net::raceopts`,
  `raceopts.rs`, `render::recolor`): a creature's body has none of the text slots the retail look's rows dress,
  so the server lists what each race's body does have, read off the disc by sight: other bodies of the same
  creature (the giants' kinds, the goblins' tribes, the aviaks' birds, the kerrans' coats, ...), a player race's
  face or hair on a head that already wears one (the satyr's, the brownie's), and parts a colour is laid on (the
  fungusman's skin, cap, growths and bag; wings, dresses, a minotaur's hide and horns; a giant's tone). The
  appearance page shows those rows in place of face and hair for such a race (each its word and the step's name,
  stepped left and right on the live model, the whole body framed; more than four scroll), and Create sends the
  steps after 0x002B's own fields (none for a race with none: 0x002B is the game's bytes). A body chosen is the
  character's model, which every client draws, a PS2 too, at its size against the race's own (the entity
  channel's scale); a colour or a face is told to clients that asked, by character name, and drawn only on the
  body it was chosen on: a part is the triangles of some palette entries within some rectangles of the texture,
  and a colour a copy of the part's texture recoloured (HSL: a dye over its own shading, or a tone that shifts its
  own colours) under a name of its own, so the rest of the body and every creature sharing the texture keep
  theirs. The first step of every row is the body as it is, byte for byte; NPCs, bodies with slots, the retail
  races and a server that sends nothing are drawn as before. `eqoa dress <body> <out.png> opts=R:a,b,c`
  (`$EQOA_RACE_OPTIONS`, a file of 0x7E23's body) draws a race in its steps.
- **The combat record**, on a server that keeps one (go-eqoa-server's offer, 0x7E20,
  and its record, 0x7E21; Sandstorm and eqoa.live have none, and show no entry): the
  start menu's Character > Combat Record, the client's own window (`record.rs`,
  `recordplay.rs`; the user, 2026-11-07: "lets add a combat record for pvp and duels to
  the menu for players, has lifetime kills, deaths, etc."): the character's lifetime
  PvP (kills, deaths, assists, the kill streak and the best, the best, last kill and
  last death) and duels (won, lost, surrendered, drawn, the tunar its wagers won and
  lost), asked for as it opens and drawn as the server words it, under a title bar as
  the game's windows are; Triangle goes back. The server's `/record` and the info
  window's lines are every client's.
- **More NPCs and players in view, and farther**: the Extended page's NPC/PC
  Display, the game's 24 up to 204, on a server that offers more
  (go-eqoa-server does; Sandstorm and eqoa.live keep the game's 24), and
  NPC/PC Distance, the game's 100 up to 1000: characters drawn that far
  on any server, and sent from that far by one that offers it.
- **Names from farther**: Characters' Name Distance, the game's 22 up to 200;
  zoomed out past the game's camera the names shrink together, to half their
  size at the farthest. Overlapping Names: Hide, the game's rule (a name whose
  plate meets a nearer one's is not drawn, so a crowd shows a few), or Show,
  every name, the nearest over the rest.
- **The GM's trigger names**, on a server that names its triggers
  (go-eqoa-server does; the user's "let vanilla show the name of the
  trigger", 2026-10-28): a GM's TEST menu shows each TRIGGER line's name
  (greyed out when the trigger is switched off, a * after it while it runs)
  where the game shows eight lines all called TRIGGER; on a server that lists
  them all (go-eqoa-server since 2026-11-09, the user's "can we add the
  triggers to client menu also in gm menus"), the TRIGGER menu is a line a
  trigger that is on, its number before its name, scrolled seven at a time as
  the game's longer menus are, each line firing its own trigger.
- **Dyes**, on a server that sends SOE's live game's look (go-eqoa-server does;
  the user's "lets use new state channel for eqoaclassic, but also have legacy
  support so sandstorm runs", 2026-10-29): the live game's state channel and
  character list carry a colour for each armour slot, held item and the robe
  where the Frontiers disc's carry one of its 15 tints, and each armour slot,
  the robe and the helm are drawn in theirs as the live client drew them on
  this disc's art (the colour in the tint's place; it had ArmorGroups this
  disc lacks). An undyed slot comes in its tint's colour and looks as it
  always has; a server that never offers the look (Sandstorm, eqoa.live) is
  read as the disc's.
- **Bug reports with F3**, on a server that takes them (go-eqoa-server's
  0x7E0E; Sandstorm and eqoa.live make no offer, and F3 does nothing there):
  Support's request form, the game's own, opened from the world on Bug Report
  with its topics open; with each petition this client files (the form, or a
  typed `/bug` or `#bug`), a note of its own first (0x7E0F, `bugnote.rs`: its
  build, the platform and graphics adapter, the frame rate, where it shows the
  player and its target), which the server keeps for the GMs beside the
  server's own view. The user, 2026-11-05: "we can add something to the
  webclient". Support's request form and its reply hold as long a text as
  the server says it takes (the offer's second u32: go-eqoa-server's 2,047
  characters, a PS2's form 511; petition 1300, 2026-11-08), sent in DRDP's
  pieces, which that server puts back together.
- **Hide UI** (H): everything drawn over the world hidden until H again or a
  window opens.
- **Ignore Shouts kept** until Restore Shouts, across logins (the game keeps
  none, and a server forgets it at the next login).
- **Several windows at once** (multiboxing): each window reads its own
  controller, the first window the first controller, the second the next, in
  front or not; the Extended page's Controller Focus picks another (windows
  that pick the same one are all driven by it), or follows the window in
  front. On Windows the Xbox controllers and the DualShock 4 and DualSense
  alike reach a window behind another.
- **Each window where it was**: natively a window opens with the size, place,
  monitor and maximized or full-screen state (F11, Alt+Enter or Video's Display) it last had, each of
  several windows its own.
- **Full screen in a browser, and the page as an app**: Start puts the page full
  screen (a phone's or a tablet's in any browser that lets a page, iOS's none, and again
  at a touch once it is left; a computer's in a Chromium-based browser, which keeps Escape the
  game's; not while multiboxing), and F11 stays the browser's own, to a window and
  back. The page installs as an app (the card's button, or the browser's menu; on an
  iPhone, whose browsers let no page go full screen, Add to Home Screen), started full
  screen with none of the browser round it.
- **The 2002 art** (F9, or the Extended page's Art): the game drawn as
  EverQuest Online Adventures looked before Frontiers, out of the player's own
  USA disc of 2002 (SLUS-20470) beside the Frontiers one: every race's body,
  face, hair, armour, helms and robes, the creatures that disc has, Tunaria's
  lighting, props and textures (with Frontiers' fog, dust, leaves, flocks and
  ambient sound kept), the windows' blue stone, the item icons, the spell
  effects, the loading pictures, the title, Station's pages and character
  select. Swapped at once, both ways, and kept. Only the art: the game, its
  words and its rules are Frontiers'.
- **PCSX2's texture packs and dumps** (the user, 2026-11-08: "Vanilla loads
  PCSX2 replacement packs as they are ... and can dump its textures under
  PCSX2's names, so one pack works in both"; `texpack.rs`, `esf::pcsx2`):
  natively, Extended > Textures. Texture Packs (off unless chosen) draws a
  texture a pack in `textures/SLUS-20744/replacements` names (PCSX2's folder
  and file names, read at any depth; PNG and DDS, its BC1/2/3/7 and
  uncompressed forms, any size) in its place: a larger one with its own
  levels, sampled that many levels farther on, so it adds detail near and
  keeps the game's texel density far; a texture off the 2002 disc looks in
  `textures/SLUS-20470`. Dump Textures (off unless chosen) writes each texture
  made to `textures/SLUS-20744/dumps` under every name PCSX2 gives it, the
  picture PCSX2 dumps byte for byte. A name is PCSX2's texture cache's own
  hash of what the game puts in the GS's memory for the surface (its levels
  as GS blocks, its CLUT), reproduced from the surface record as
  `VIEESurfCache` uploads it; held to PCSX2's dumps of the game (below, the
  `esf` row). Off, every texture is the game's own, byte for byte; a change to
  either row makes every texture again (`App::retexture`). The `textures`
  folder is beside the program (the user, 2026-11-09: "lets put the
  dumps/replacements in a folder next to the binary, so players can find it
  easy"; `texpack::place`): the program's own folder (the release's archive's
  or its bare download's), a Mac app's the folder holding the `.app`, an
  AppImage's the folder of `$APPIMAGE` (the release has neither). Where the
  game cannot write there (Program Files, /usr/bin, a read-only mount) it is
  the config directory's, as before, and the page says so; on Android always.
  The config directory's from before is moved over the first time it is
  wanted (one rename: on the same disk only), else read after the new one.
  The updater renames only the program, so the folder outlives updates. On a
  computer Texture Folder (Open) shows the folder in the system's file manager
  (Explorer, `open`, `xdg-open`), and the page's note says where it is; a run
  that keeps no settings (the test commands) has no folder. Packs load off the
  frame (the owner, 2026-11-09: "lets make texture packs load async"): the
  folder is read on a thread (every file's header with it), and each stand-in
  is read and decoded on the pack's own thread the first time it is wanted;
  its texture is made at once at its final size with the surface's own levels
  drawn into it by the GPU (`render::standin`), and the read levels are
  written into that same texture at a frame's start (8 MB a frame), so no bind
  group or render bundle is made again. Arriving at Freeport with 570
  stand-ins 4x the size (317 MB decoded) froze the old build ~4-6 s; now the
  worst frame is ~50 ms. A shot reads them as it asks (`texpack::sync_now`),
  and `EQOA_TEXTURES_SYNC=1` does so in a live run, to measure against.
  A dump is always the surface's own record's texture, never a stand-in.
- **Sorted lists**: the Who list and the guild's roster by name, or by any
  column with ○ (C on the keyboard) or a click on its heading (the game keeps
  the server's order).
- **Network Setup** on the title: the address of the server to play on (on a
  PS2 it starts the console's network utility).
- **Updates**: a downloaded build puts the release's newer one in its place
  as it starts, before the title.

## Playing a release

One build is published, **dev**: rebuilt from `master` on every push, for Linux
(x86-64), Windows (x86-64), macOS and Android, on the
[releases page](https://github.com/DabDavis/eqoa-rust-client-vanilla/releases/tag/dev).
Each build replaces the one before, so only the newest can be downloaded; the
release page's update notes (`UPDATE-NOTES.md`) say what each build changed.

On Android (8.0 and later: phones, tablets, Chromebooks) it is
`eqoa-vanilla-android.apk`: open the download to install it, allowing the browser
or the file manager to install apps when Android asks. It does not update itself
(an installed app cannot replace itself): a newer build installs over the old one
the same way, its settings kept, as both carry the project's key. The first time it
asks for the disc image in Android's own file picker, so copy the image to the
device first; it keeps the pick. It plays as the other builds do, the touch
screen's controller there from the start, a controller or a keyboard as on a
computer, and the device's keyboard for the game's text boxes (README "Android").

The program's icon is a drawn disc of its own (`icon_art.rs`: no game art; the
Windows `.exe` carries it in its resources, made by `build.rs` from the same
drawing, and the pages have it as `web/disc.svg`) until it has read the
player's disc image; from then on it is the game's own memory card icon, the
3D head a PS2's browser showed for the save, drawn from SUPPORT's
`VIMemCardIconData` on the player's disc (`saveicon.rs`) and kept as
`saveicon.png` in the config directory, so the next start with that disc opens
with it (`icon.rs`: the window under Windows and X11, the Dock on a Mac;
Wayland shows a program's icon only through a desktop entry, for which the
Linux archive carries `eqoa.png`). `eqoa icon <size> <out.png> [save]` writes
either.

It is the `eqoa` program, in an archive with its README and licence, or on its
own (`eqoa-vanilla-linux-x86_64`, `eqoa-vanilla-windows-x86_64.exe`,
`eqoa-vanilla-macos`: on Linux and macOS `chmod +x` it after the download). You
need your own *Frontiers* disc image (SLUS-20744) and an account on a server
(or make one on Station's login screen, where the server allows it). Start
`eqoa` (`eqoa.exe` on Windows; `./eqoa` on Linux and macOS). The first time, it
asks for the disc image in the desktop's own file dialog, whatever the image is
called and wherever it is, checks that it is *Frontiers* (by the program its
SYSTEM.CNF starts, as the page does) and keeps its place for the next time.
`eqoa /path/to/image.iso`, or the image dropped on `eqoa.exe`, plays and keeps
that image instead, and `EQOA_ISO=/path/to/image.iso` names one for a run. The
game starts as the console does: the loading screens, the legal screen and the
licence, then the title. On the title, **Network Setup** takes the server's
address (a host name or an IPv4 address, kept for the next time); **Play**
brings Station's login screen (your account and password; Save Account keeps
both, the password in plain text, as the memory card kept them), the server's
news, the server list, character select (with creation on an empty slot) and
the world. Quitting from the start menu goes back to the server list, as on a
PS2. `eqoa boot <server>` starts with that server instead of the kept one, and
`eqoa play <server> <account>` (password in `EQOA_PASSWORD`, `EQOA_CHAR=<name>`)
goes straight into the world, for scripts. The game's options, the keys, the
server and the disc image's place (`disc.opt`, its path alone) are kept in
`eqoa-rs` in the config directory (`~/.config/eqoa-rs`, `~/Library/Application
Support/eqoa-rs`, `%APPDATA%\eqoa-rs`). Several windows open at once each read
their own controller (the first window the first; the Extended page's Controller
Focus picks another, the same for several, or the window in front; on Windows a
controller that is not an Xbox one, or passed off as one, reaches only the window
in front). On Windows `eqoa.exe` opens no console window: started from a terminal,
its messages print there. On Linux it needs glibc 2.34 or newer
(Ubuntu 22.04, Debian 12, Fedora 35 or later), ALSA (`libasound2`), libudev and a
Vulkan or OpenGL driver; the file dialog is the desktop's (its XDG
desktop portal, else zenity), and where there is neither the image is named on
the command line. On a Steam Deck in Steam's Game Mode (gamescope, which shows
no desktop portal's window) a disc not known or not there (one kept on a USB
drive, which Game Mode does not mount) is first looked for where a Deck keeps
its games (the home folder and its Downloads, Desktop, Documents, Games and ROMs
folders, EmuDeck's `Emulation/roms/ps2`, and the same on the microSD card under
`/run/media`, a folder down), then asked for in zenity's dialog. The builds are not
signed: the first time, macOS refuses one that came through a browser; allow it
in System Settings, Privacy & Security ("Open Anyway"), or run `xattr -d
com.apple.quarantine eqoa`. Windows' SmartScreen may ask the first time ("More
info", "Run anyway").

**The 2002 art.** With your own *EverQuest Online Adventures* (USA) disc image
of 2002 (SLUS-20470) beside the *Frontiers* one, **F9** (or Options > Extended >
Art) draws the game as it looked then, anywhere from the loading screens to the
world; F9 again goes back. The first time, F9 at the title or at character
select asks for that image in the file dialog and keeps its place
(`disc2002.opt`), and the choice is kept too (`EQOA_2002_ISO=/path/to/image.iso`
names one for a run). In the world it says so in the chat instead: the dialog
holds the window while it is open, and a server lets a silent session go within
seconds. Without the disc nothing changes: a kept image that is gone counts as
none, and one that does not open leaves Frontiers' world. The European discs of
2003 already carry nearly all of Frontiers' art and the 2002 betas lay their
files out apart, so only the USA disc is taken. What only Frontiers has (its new
creatures and worlds, its new icons) stays as Frontiers draws it.

**A downloaded build updates itself.** As it starts (`eqoa`, `eqoa boot`, `eqoa
<image>`), a thread reads the release's `update.txt`, which the release's build
writes beside its programs: the commit it was built from, and each platform's
program with its SHA-256 and size. When the commit is not this build's, the
program is downloaded beside this one, held to that hash and size, and put in
its place by two renames (this one aside as `eqoa.old`, which the next start
removes; on Windows, while another window of the game still runs the one the last
update set aside, which nothing may remove or replace, as `eqoa.old-1` and on); the
boot's loading picture says how far the download is, and before
the title the window closes and the new build starts in its place, with the
same arguments. A build made anywhere but the release's CI carries no channel
and never updates itself; `EQOA_NO_UPDATE=1` leaves a release's build as it is
for a run, and a failure (no network, a folder it may not write to, a download
that is not the one named) is said in the log and the game plays on. While the
game runs it asks the release again (three minutes after the start, then every
ten; the release, not the game server, so on any server), puts a newer build in
place the same way, and asks the player whether to restart now in a window of
the game's own that lists what is new in it (the sections of the release's
update notes this build's own lack, every version's since if several came out),
once nothing else is on the screen, nothing is being typed and the controls have
been let go a moment: Restart now leaves the world as Quit does and starts the
new build; Later (the choice it starts on) asks again in half an hour. A page
asks the same of its player (Reload / Later), with what is new in it.

**The program's messages are kept** in `eqoa.log` in the config directory (the
run before as `eqoa.prev.log`; a second window open beside the first keeps
`eqoa-2.log`, and so on); a frame that came a second or more after the last is
one of them (`stall.rs`: how long, when in UTC, the frame's longest phases or the
time outside it, the game's waits for the sound's driver, the disc's slowest read,
the game server's last datagram), as is each window's coming to the front
(`arrival.rs`), and a fault's message and backtrace. In a page the browser's
console is the log.

## Method

The language is not what makes the feel exact; the method is. Each system is ported
from the matching decomp ([eqoa-decomp](https://github.com/DabDavis/eqoa-decomp)) one at
a time and proved against a reference:

- **Game logic** (walking, camera, collision, animation) uses the EE's float model,
  crate `ee`: round toward zero, denormals flushed on the way in and out, saturation
  instead of infinity, conditioned compares, an adder with one guard bit. Every rule
  but the last was measured in a running game through the tracer (`go-eqoa-vi`); the
  tracer runs PCSX2's interpreter, whose adder keeps every bit, and the guard bit is
  PCSX2's recompiler's, which the game plays on. Rust never fuses `a*b+c` on its own,
  so written order is executed order.
- **The reference** is eqoa-port, which runs the game's own functions on the host: a
  test gives the original and the rewrite the same inputs and compares the bits.
  Until that harness exists, crates are held to the fixtures the earlier ports were.
- **Rendering** need not be bit-exact. The VU1 microcode is fully read, and eqoa-port's
  GPU strip programs already redo its maths as shaders; meshes are drawn directly with
  that maths, with no DMA, VIF or GS.

## First slice

A test of the feel before anything broad: load a zone and the player's character off
the disc, port `VIWalkControl` and `VIWalkCamera`, draw with the strip-program shaders,
and compare against eqoa-port frame by frame. If that matches, the rest is breadth:
UI, network (DRDP), sound.

## Crates

| crate | what | checked against |
|---|---|---|
| `ee` | the EE FPU: `mul` `add` `sub` `div` `sqrt` `lt` `le` `eq`; and `ee::libm`, newlib as SUPPORT has it compiled: `sinf` `cosf` `powf` (with its `_XOPEN_` wrapper) `atanf` `atan2f` (wrapper too) `acosf` `sqrtf` `fmodf` `scalbnf`. The adder aligns the smaller operand with one guard bit and no sticky bit, as PCSX2's recompiler models the EE (`FPU_ADD_SUB`): bits shifted past the guard are dropped first, and an operand 25 or more exponents below adds only its sign, so `x + -tiny` is `x`. go-eqoa-vi's goldens were measured through the tracer's `call`, which runs PCSX2's interpreter, whose adder keeps every bit (the exact sum toward zero): that one is `ee::interp` (with `interp::libm`, the same newlib over it), kept for the ports checked against it. With it a body creeping on a trace of velocity moved a float step toward the world's origin every walk tick for twenty seconds after a stop (2026-10-20, F3: the slide). Computed natively and branch-free, inlined into the game logic (about half a million calls a frame in play, more than half with a zero operand): the f64 product or sum truncated to 24 bits by a mask, the alignment's masks by selects; `mul4`/`add4` take four lanes at once (`Matrix44::mul`, `apply`): on SSE2 on x86_64 (the alignment's shifts as a float's conversion), on NEON on aarch64 (phones, tablets, Apple's Macs: the shifts by a register) and on SIMD128 on wasm32 built with it (the page, `web/build.sh`: the shifts as SSE2's, its conversion unsigned and saturating), one lane at a time elsewhere | goldens from `go-eqoa-vi`; `interp::libm` bit for bit against go-eqoa-vi's ports over a 24,000-input corpus (`tools/vimath-corpus` writes it), except `atan2f(denormal, ±0)`, where the wrapper's `c.eq.s` flushes the denormal (disassembly at 0x0012711C) and the Go does not, and `libm` where the adders part (`cosf(pi)` is -1 exactly); and against its first implementation, kept verbatim in `tests/reference.rs` (the reference for `interp`), and `FPU_ADD_SUB` written out step for step (for `add`/`sub`): `sqrt` and `operand` over all 2^32 patterns, the binary operations and compares over 1,592 chosen values pairwise and random pairs in every regime the shortcuts branch on (`EE_PAIRS=100000000` checked 500M), four lanes against four calls (NEON's under `qemu-aarch64` too, 400M lanes, and on GitHub's arm64 machines; SIMD128's as WASI programs under node, the suite on one thread there, 400M lanes); five planted shortcut bugs and a bound one exponent too loose each fail it, and the alignment's edge one exponent off (scalar and four-lane) or without its partial mask, and eight planted in NEON's (the edge, a kept bit, each range test's boundary, the truncation, the exponent-255 clamp, lanes swapped, masks swapped), ten in SIMD128's (the same, its conversion signed, its high lanes widened from the low). `cargo run --release -p ee --example lanes` times four lanes against one at a time |
| `iso` | ISO 9660 walk, read one file | the disc |
| `dnas` | The disc's DNAS-encrypted modules (the `.AUT`s: SUPPORT, CLIENT, SETUP, STATION, MCERR) unpacked as `VILauncher::UnpackAndVerify` unpacks them, by the disc's own DNAS library: `UTIL.REL` (on the disc in the clear) loaded and relocated as SN's DLL loader does it, its imports (libc, the kernel's semaphores, libcdvd's S-command RPC) bound to stand-ins, and an interpreter for the EE's integer core running its constructors, its init and the four personalize and extract calls (each word decoded once, the library's self-decrypting code decoded again when it is rewritten); then the inflate. The standard functions the library spends half its instructions in are computed natively (`src/native.rs`): SHA-1's compression, RC6-32/20 and BSAFE's CBC decrypt over it, libgcc's `__muldi3`, `dpmul`, `litodp` and `dptoul`, BSAFE's bignum multiply-accumulate and the stream's buffer reader, each only where its code's CRC-32 is the proved one (else interpreted) and free to decline a call to the interpreter; DNAS's own state machine stays interpreted. No key and no part of the library is in the code. About 110M interpreted instructions a module: 1.2 s natively (5.5 s interpreting all 320M), 1.9-2.7 s in Chromium; the client unpacks CLIENT and SUPPORT on threads of their own (a page's workers) and keeps them (`eqoa/src/modules.rs`) | `tests/discs.rs`: every module with a reference on hand, byte for byte against what the game's own `UnpackAndVerify` returned in PCSX2: the Frontiers disc's SUPPORT and CLIENT through its ISO, and 30 more off six other discs (EQOA's 2002-10-20 beta, USA, Europe, Europe En/Fr/De and its demo, the Frontiers beta of 2003-07-18); one bit flipped in a `.AUT`'s header, body or trailer is refused by the library itself. `tests/natives.rs`: every native call of two whole unpacks (246,000 and 250,000) against the interpreter running the function, the registers a call keeps, its result and every byte either wrote (an undo log), and 200,000 calls of the pure ones on random arguments; a native wrong in its result, its bytes or by a stray write fails it |
| `esf` | CSF inflate, the OBJF record tree, DictIDs, surfaces (and their mip levels, `tests/mips.rs`), PrimBuffers (a flora sprite's 0x1230 too, `ParseFloraPrimBuffer`'s packings: every one of ODUS's 182 inside its header's box, `tests/flora.rs`), materials, sprites flattened to models; `Library`, the dictionary across files; zones (terrain with pre-translations, actors); a world opened with its zones left on the disc (`Container::open_deferred` through a `ReadAt`: the root and the world walked header by header, every zone a stub naming its byte range; TUNARIA is 20,772 bytes read in 180 reads instead of 997 MB), a zone parsed on its own (`parse_records`), and `Library::add_zone`, which registers a zone's DictIDs at its world's place (ranked by the record's offset), so lookups answer as with the whole file loaded; PCSX2's names for a surface (`pcsx2`: TEX0Hash, the XXH3-64 of its levels as the GS's memory holds them, PSMT8's and PSMT4's blocks swizzled; CLUTHash; TEX0's bits) and the picture PCSX2 dumps of it | PCSX2's own dumps of the game in the tracer (`tests/pcsx2.rs` with `$EQOA_PCSX2_DUMPS`, best `--release`: of 571 dumps from the licence to Freeport, 537 named by a surface, 198 surfaces of 12 files, 8- and 4-bit, 16 to 512 a side, mip chains and their dumped levels, every one's pixels the surface's; the other 34 are the fonts' glyph caches, filled as the game runs and named by nothing on the disc); `go-eqoa-esf-tools-v2`: per-surface CRCs (801 surfaces) and per-model geometry (766 models: counts, bbox bits, position CRC); which records register a DictID, and that the first file loaded wins, from the decomp; the deferred open's world-level records, and each zone parsed alone, against the whole parse record for record (`tests/deferred.rs`; a changed byte and a range one byte late fail) |
| `vi` | the game logic, ported from the decomp and generic over a float model: `VIWalkControl`, `VIWalkCamera`, `VICameraEffect`; and **`VIScene::Collide`**: the world and zone BSPs, rooms, collision buffers (all three packings), placed actors and their insertion, Simple/Sub/Group/LOD sprites, `GetEnvFlags` and the liquid-surface ray, loaded from the world file as `VIESFParse` loads it (`world.rs`). Zones come later (`World::add_zone`): a world whose library has only some zones builds those, and each one added inserts the actors already in that reach into it; every room it touched is put back in the whole load's insertion order (zone, room, placement), so a walk meets the same actors in the same order however the zones came. Append only (no `ResetToProxy` yet). Every actor names a sprite its own zone holds (all six worlds, `esf/examples/zone_dups.rs`). The actors made in play (`CreateActor`, `SetActorTransform`, `SetActorSprite`, `SetActorCollideable`, `ReleaseActor`: the creatures) go into the rooms they reach and out of those they leave (`RemoveActor`), after each room's own actors, and a `VICSprite` collides as its capsule (`SetDefaults`' `m_capsule`: from the feet 2 up, 0.5 round, in the actor's space), as nothing while its copy is dead, never with a ray | **bit for bit against the decomp compiled for the host** (eqoa-port's `make oracle`), under the `Ieee` model: 18 walk-control and 11 camera methods, and `VICameraEffect`'s manual camera (`Apply` with its slerped blend, `BeginManual`, `EndManual`, `Update`; ops 250-253); `VIMatrix44` Transform/InvertAffine/Mul, `VIPlane`, the transformed `VICollSphere::Init`, the triangle/edge/vertex sweeps, and `CollideObj` over 102 real TUNARIA buffers; the BSP walk against brute force over every room and actor (3,000 sweeps). Negative controls fail; zone by zone (`tests/world_zones.rs`: the zones within 1,050 of Freeport added in a scrambled order) against the same zones at once and against the whole file: room lists, 3,000 floors, sweeps, rays and environment flags bit for bit; a reversed room and a zone left out fail; the actors made in play (`tests/world_actors.rs`): a signpost stops a walk's sweep at its capsule grown by the sweep's radius, and not beside it, over it, through the floor or camera filters, as a ray, dead, not collideable, moved away or released; a creature walking 600 m across Freeport is in exactly the rooms a fresh actor there would be in, and the walk meets it as brute force does (a walk that skips them and an `UpdateActor` that leaves them in their old rooms both fail) |

A mote born on frame 60 reads `sDustCard[60]`, past the table (the oracle's build has `sFogCard[0]` there); `Set(x, y, z)`'s three draws in `VIBoid::Init` are evaluated last argument first, as the oracle's compiler does. a character's sprite has none); each lifted 0.04, its UV the local box's, its alpha `(1 - clamp(feet - y, 0, 1)) * 0.65`; `RasterActor`'s queue (a CSprite actor rastered within 50 of the eye, `m_farPlane`'s 2500 squared, 128 at most; `shadows.rs`) in the order `VIScene::Render`'s walk rasters the actors (`World::raster_actors`, `RenderByPortals` down to `RasterActor`'s tests: from the camera's room, outdoors the world by the frustum, indoors the room and each room a portal in view leads into, under the volume the clipped portal makes, `vi::clipstack`'s `VIClipStack` and `VIFrustum`; the rooms' boxes and portals as `ParseZoneRoom` reads them, each portal's plane by `CalcPortalPlane`), so a character behind a wall casts none | **bit for bit against the decomp**: eqoa-port's `oracle_shadow` (op 1600) compiles viscene.cpp over a recording raster and runs RenderShadow itself (314 cases, 7,888 triangles, every raster call's floats; a node moved 0.01 the control); the actor walk against brute force on the disc (600 boxes round Freeport; the rooms alone the control); `VIClipStack` and `CalcPlanes` bit for bit against eqoa-port's ops 1700 and 1701 (200 frusta, 150 scripted sessions: the frustum pushed, spheres and boxes culled, portals clipped, one with a corner on a plane, a clipped portal pushed and tested under); on TUNARIA every portal as its record has it (2,662), and the walk from inside closed rooms (of 112 views that stayed inside, the frustum alone took actors behind the walls in 101) and from outdoors (every one of 395 actors of the camera's own room in view rastered); a caster behind a wall queued by the frustum alone, the control, and not by the walk |

The manifest is `data\psvrpath.dat`'s path on port 7000 of the server Network Setup keeps (`EQOA_PATCH_PORT` for a test server), fetched with `VUrl`'s exact request (GET with Host alone) and judged by its rules: Content-Length mandatory, case-sensitive and met; every file gzipped (`Http_get`: the header skipped, raw deflate); a file judged by SIZE unless the distribution says UseCRC, fetched whole, its CRC checked after every download; deletes after downloads; a Restart file cancelling the rest; nothing written unless all of it succeeded. The card is the server's own folder (`servers.rs`: `<config>/servers/<host>/`, the files flat as the PS2's `BASLUS-20744EQONLINE` holds them, and `patch.json`, the last finished patch's record): each server keeps its own, the user's word (2026-10-23). Past the patch the game's words are the folder's WORDS.TXT (`Res::set_server_words`), the disc's again at the title. The page hands the game its host's name, not its address, so the https fetch has a certificate to match. A server whose web server does not serve its patch files is unreachable, and its folder is played as it stands (the manifest answered with an error, a 404 page as eqoaclassic.com's, or no answer in 10 s, as natively a patch port that does not connect in 10 s; a listed file the site lacks still fails the patch); only the play that stands is logged, the plays before it answering what was not fetched yet with nothing. The run speed is the server's as `UpdateGame` takes it: the dump's `m_speed`, then 0x00F8's and Class Mastery's 5 and 6 through `SetSpeed` (its walk speed 3, or 0 with a run speed of 0), 1.05 times it while following (Sandstorm: 8.833333, 11.483333 under Spirit of the Wolf); until 2026-10-23 the walk kept its own 8.83 and no speed buff moved it. Creatures are solid as the game makes them: every entity is an actor in the collision world (`VIEntity::CreateActor`, placed where it is drawn as `Extrapolate` places it, `Play::place_actors`; the player's own, `place_self_actor`, at the walk's committed place), of its model's sprite whether that draws anything or not (`VIClientPart06D00::LoadRes`; model 0 is the null sprite, nothing), so the walk stops at players, NPCs and signposts (an NPC with a character's model) and slides round them, at a character's capsule grown by the walk's sphere (`VICSprite::Collide`, the walk's `VISceneFilter` passing over only the player's own actor); the floor and camera probes pass through them, a corpse lets the walk through, and so does a creature the ignore list names (`RefreshCollision`, again at 0x1003-0x1005); the other players' walks stop at the player and at each other the same way; until 2026-10-24 the walk went through every creature. A creature with nothing to draw is solid too: Sandstorm's blockers are CHAR.ESF's 0x6F412342, a SimpleSprite with an empty prim buffer and a collision buffer, a closed box 5 a side about the entity's place (times its scale) whose faces point out, so a walk stops at it from outside and leaves it freely from inside; until 2026-10-29 such an entity had no actor here (the actor was made with the drawn body) and players walked through them (F3 20261028-233048). Server-driven actions (0x0775) and the level and experience (0x0020). Groups: `InviteEntity` (0x0625), the invitation's prompt (0x0773), `AcceptInvite`/`DeclineInvite`, leave/remove/disband, the roster (0x0772) and the members' health and place (state channel 0x40). Summons and resurrections: 0x00D3's Yes/No box (`Msg00D3`: the caster's name, then word 610 or 611, run together as the console runs them, put up over whatever window was open), answered with 0x00D4 (`SendD4On`/`SendD4Off`). Abilities: the dump's list kept by 0x00B3/B4/B5/B6/B8/CD with `Msg00B4`..`Msg00CD`'s rules, used from the HUD's menus as `Send0032` (token, slot, target). Keys are the game's own keyboard (`VIWndGame::OnKeyDown`/`OnKeyUp`, `VIWndHUDMenu::OnKeyDown`, the codes `VIKeyboard::Read` gives) where they do not collide with walking on WASD, the user's choice (2026-10-20): WASD walks (the client's), the arrows are the quick menus' d-pad (up and down the selection, left and right the menu) and E uses it (the game has W/S/A/D on the quick menus and the arrows walking), Home and End orbit the follow camera, Page Up and Page Down pitch or zoom (`UpdateControls`' `m_keys[21..=24]`, `controls::Client::keys`); Space attacks, Tab targets the next creature, ` or ~ cycles the player, the group and the pet (L1's `CycleTarget(1)`), 1 to 6 pick the player, the group's others and the pet (`SelectTarget`), Q talks, X toggles autorun, C held scrolls the chat box's history (as R2), Enter (as R3) or `/` opens the say box, the edit box R3 opens (`/` begun with "/"), R and T with `/reply` and `/tell` (`Play::chat_key`; a plain line before the HUD is up); Z opens the quick-chat menus as L2 does (below: the arrows are their directions there, held to edit, Enter the chat line and in the editor X, as E is, Escape, Backspace or Z back out). The client's own beside them: I/J/K/L a right stick, F5-F8 the group's actions, F11 full screen (natively the window opens as it was last left: its size, place, monitor, maximized or full screen, kept per window in the config directory's `window.opt`/`window-<n>.opt` by `winplace.rs`, a place no monitor shows any more dropped, F3 20261024-063145), F1 the options' Controls page (the row "the options": every key here can be moved to another there, and its last lines say which key is which button in a window: ✕ Enter or E, △ Escape or Backspace, □ Q, so a trade's Confirm Trade is Q; in the corpse's window Space is ✕ too, as the attack that opened it is on the pad, F3 20261029-024647).
