| the mouse and the touch screen | The PS2 reads neither; these are the client's, laid over the game's inputs. In play, the mouse (`mouse.rs`) is World of Warcraft's (the user's choice, F3 2026-10-21): a held button that drags takes the pointer (a page's Pointer Lock, asked for at the drag, within the press's activation, so a click never hides the cursor, and in the press itself once a browser has refused it twice running, `Mouse::lock_seen`; a refusal is a line in the log, not a problem notice: `web/main.js` catches the promise winit drops, F3 20261023-193535; natively winit's `Locked`), the cursor hidden and the look on the mouse's own motion (`DeviceEvent::MouseMotion`: a page's `movementX`, natively the device's counts), so no screen edge stops it; where there is no lock (X11, Windows) the pointer is `Confined` to the window, the cursor's moves are the look and it is put back to the window's middle as it nears an edge (`Mouse::recentre`: under X11's grab every raw motion comes twice); let go, the cursor is back where the press was; a window or the start menu opening, a lost focus, Escape (which a browser keeps to end its lock) and a death let go of it, clicking nothing. The left button looks round the orbiting cameras without turning the body (across the orbit angle, the view turning the way the mouse goes; up and down the tilt, from 45 degrees under the head to 75 overhead (`mouse::tilt`: in mode 3 the default pitch its own snaps go back to, so near a wall it still takes the game's alternate pitch); low, the ground pulls the camera in as the game's sweep pulls it in from anything, only as far as the ground needs, and it looks up past the body at the sky). The right button steers: in the follow orbit the body turns to the view as the hold begins, then the body and the camera behind it turn together, and the walk is on the body's own axes (`controls::Client::steer`, the client's: W and S forward and back, A and D a sidestep at the game's walk speed with its strafe animation, as first person walks; the camera is put back behind every frame, since the game re-derives the orbit from where the camera was, and where the game would swing it round to a clear angle it comes in instead, only as far as what is in the way needs (`camreach::come_in`; it came in to its closest until 2026-11-09, which in Freeport's well took it all the way in to the character); the mouse's heading wins over the fight's turn to the target); while the player moves (the keys, a stick, autorun: `Mouse::moving`) the left button in the follow orbit steers as the right does (`Mouse::held_as`, F3 20261025-044637), standing still it looks round again; both buttons run forward, steered, A and D adding a sidestep (the diagonal). A body the mouse turns takes its run round with it, on a floor or in a liquid (the velocity's forward and sideways parts put on the new heading's axes, its fall kept; F3 20261025-163410, jib's, the user's choice): the game's walk had braked against the old run, backed, stood and walked before running the new way, about 300 ms at 30 frames a second after a half turn; sticks and keys turn as the game turns. In first person and the follow camera either button turns the body at once (`mouse::set_heading`: full circles) and pitches it within the walk's range (in first person 89.4 degrees up and down, the game's `PITCH_LIMIT`; at a liquid's surface the pitch is the stick's dive). The Camera Pitch Axis option turns the pitch round (in the orbits the orbit too), as it does the stick's; with no button held the keys are as they were. The wheel zooms the follow orbit, in from where the camera is and out from the distance chosen, past the closest into first person (as P's style 0) and out of it back to the camera it left, at its distance and tilt (`mouse::Zoom`, `camstyle::zoom_in`/`zoom_out`, `Play::wheel_style`); the follow orbit zooms out to 11, the client's, where the game stops at 8 (`camstyle::FOLLOW_MAX_DISTANCE`). A wall pulls the follow orbit in as the game's sweep does (`MoveCamera`'s flag 4, at 23.5 a second, to 3.5, then its closest) and the game keeps that distance; here (`camreach`, the client's; F3 20261025-060553, the user's: "only zoom what is needed then zoom back out to where it was") a pull stops 0.1 in front of what is in the way when that is farther out than 3.5 (the sweep's sphere keeps it 0.5 off the surface; pulled again only once the obstacle comes 0.1 nearer, so it does not rock), and the camera eases back out toward the distance chosen as far as the way is clear (the distance the wheel, the stick's zoom or a style chose; from rest to at most 12 a second, 3.5 to 11 in about 1.3 s), following an obstacle out as the body moves; within 3.5 everything is the game's (the pull to 3.5, the alternate pitch, the angle hunt, its closest), and with nothing in the way the camera is the game's bit for bit. Something thin in the way moves it not at all (`camreach::PastThin`, the client's; F3 2026-11-09, the user, zoomed out in Freeport's well: "the posts collision make the cam zoom"): every sweep the camera makes goes on past a post, a pole or a beam narrower than its sphere is across (nothing within 0.5 to either side of where the sweep met it, a far side within 0.6 behind it), while a wall, a roof's underside, a broad trunk or a wall just behind a post stops it as before. A click targets the creature under it (`Play::creature_at`: of the creatures whose upright from the feet to the name plate passes within a quarter of its screen height, the nearest), a double or right click talks to it (`InteractWithTarget`) or opens its corpse; on the HUD a click uses a quick menu's row (its title bar the next menu), opens the chat line from the chat box (as R3), and targets the player, a group member or the pet from their frames (as L1's cycle). On the windows (`pointer.rs`, `Play::point`) a pointer is the keys each window takes. Every button glyph drawn is that button: the HUD's raster notes where each of the atlas's button glyphs went, with the words drawn after it (`EQOA_GLYPHS=1` prints them), so every legend ("✕ Select  △ Exit", "R2 End", "(R1)") is clickable; the side arrow (atlas 128) is not one, it points in as often as out. A window notes where it drew its rows, tabs and fields (`pointer::Hits`): a click selects a row (the move's sound) and a click on the selection uses it, as ✕; a popup's row, an option list's, a message's choice and a box's button are used at once, and a click off a popup closes it (a box and an editor keep it: what was typed is not lost); tabs, pages and the other of two lists step there with the keys that switch them; a value changed with left and right (the options' sliders, the trainer's points, Class Mastery's pages) takes a click's side of it; a digit picker puts its cursor on the digit, and above or below it is up or down; where a window's own move does more than move (the trainer's item list, the options' page rows) the click presses up or down to get there. A right click is △, the wheel (a notch a step) and a finger's drag are up and down. A pointer's keys go through a queue, one a frame, each held until the HUD's next frame has drawn: the windows' `KeyFlash` acts only on a key still down when it draws. Every window has it: the start menu and its sub-menus, the inventory (tabs, rows and their popups, the quick-slot column, the quantity picker, the destroy box), the spell book (abilities, level tabs, slots, popup), the who list, the buddy and ignore lists, the guild roster, the options (the Controls page's rows and cells, a click on the selection waiting for its key, any click giving up), the quest journal, the trainer, Class Mastery, the factions, the tradeskill windows, the corpse, the merchant, trading (its three lists, the money and quantity pickers), the bank and the auction (sell page, search form, results, bids), mail (compose, inbox, reader), tells, the blacksmith, the customer-service forms, dialogs, boxes, the edit box's soft keyboard. The touch screen (`touch.rs`): an on-screen controller in the game's own glyphs (SUPPORT's atlas: the face buttons, L1-R2, Start, Select, L3, R3, the d-pad's arrows), one more source on the pad's port, so it does all a pad does, laid out as a DualShock 4 (the user, 2026-10-27: "in landscape have ps4 controller layout, aka dual analog"): two floating sticks low and in from the sides (a finger put down near one is its centre; the left walks, the right turns the camera, as a controller's do), the d-pad above the left one and the face buttons above the right one, L2 L1 and R1 R2 over those (under the HUD's compass and bars), Select and Start at the top in the middle, L3 and R3 just over their sticks; in a window taller than wide the same controller along the bottom, Select, Start, L3 and R3 between the sticks. Sized by the window's height, less where it is too narrow for the sticks to keep apart, so every shape keeps all of it on the screen. Extended > Controller has it drawn otherwise (a player's ask of 2026-11-02 and Jibrish's petition 1386 of 2026-11-09, "Mobile clients and mobile browser need the ability to adjust the on screen controler size"; `touch::Opts`, client.opt's `touch_stick`, `touch_buttons`, `touch_opacity`, `touch_edge`, each written only when not the pad as it was, which is drawn to the pixel when they are left as they come): Touch Stick Size and Touch Button Size, 50 to 200% of the pad as it was in fives, Touch Opacity, 10 to 200% of its alphas in tens (at most opaque), and Touch Edge, 0 to 10% of the window's shorter side in from its left and right edges. They took over Touch Pad Size (the whole pad's 50 to 100%, an older client.opt's `touch_size`, which gives both sizes and is then drawn as it was). Both sizes at most the pad's and no edge, it is the pad drawn smaller toward where it is held, each half toward its bottom corner and Select and Start (taller than wide, L3 and R3 too) toward the middle of their edge, as Touch Pad Size drew it. Otherwise each part is where the full pad has it at its own size (a larger one grown in place: the shoulders under the HUD, the clusters under them, a stick from its bottom and outer gaps; the sides' parts the edge farther in), then moved as far as it needs to keep off the others (wider than tall, each stick no farther out than its cluster, the cluster's middle above the stick's, the stick and its L3 then in toward the middle; taller than wide, L3, R3, Select and Start between the sticks up off them, the clusters over all of them, the shoulders over the clusters: `touch::place`); and where that leaves something off the screen, past the edge, meeting another, up in the HUD wider than tall or above the window's lower 60% taller than wide, the sizes past what fits come down together, the larger first, as far as they must (`touch::layout`), which the page says beside Back ("Buttons drawn at 126% here"). Grown alike everywhere the full pad was already the most that fits (grown by a tenth, a shoulder meets the HUD wider than tall and L3 or Select a stick upright); so moved, a phone held wide (about 2.2 to 1) takes the sticks at 200% and the buttons at 167%, both at once about 146%, a 16:9 screen 164% and 143%, a tablet held wide 126% and about 120% (at Touch Edge's 10% it is drawn at 95%), a phone held upright 145% and 127%, a tablet upright 140% and 123%. A finger's slop and a double tap's spread stay the full pad's at any size. Over a window and on the game's screens before the world (Station's soft keyboard and its R2 End among them) every button stays the pad's, drawn fainter, and a stick takes only a finger put down inside its ring: a finger anywhere else is the window's, a tap as a click (missing by up to six frame pixels) and a drag scrolling it; over the quick-chat menus a tap on a choice is that direction tapped, and a mouse press on a choice holds its direction until it is let go (a click is a tap, half a second the editor); a finger dragged elsewhere in play looks as the mouse does, two pinch the orbit's distance, a tap targets and a double tap talks. The start menu (Escape in play, the character framed by `ApplyRaceCamera` from CLIENT's `gRaceCameras`: `VIWndStartMenu`'s frame (its band, rails and side panels run off a TV's sides; in a window wider than 4:3 it stays the game's 4:3 picture in the middle, band and all (`Anchor::Frame`), the HUD at the window's sides beside it, compass and all (the user's, 2026-10-23: from 2026-10-20 its panels went out to the window's sides), and a post closes each of its edges, the client's: the middle frame's studded side from the screen's top down to the bottom rail's corner, `startmenu::close_sides`; and it ends at its bottom rail's bar, the client's: the rail's last six rows fade into the full-screen backdrop's teal, and are left out with that backdrop (`ui::Cut`, `startmenu::RAIL_BAR`; F3 20261023-084953, the user: "cut off the rest under the bottom horizontal frame"), the spell book, the inventory's posts and the merchant's frame the same; the inventory over it stays in the picture too, the post drawn again over its own left panel; and so does the spell book, whose side panels run off a TV's sides too, its top the start menu's band (the game's own backdrop shows there round its tabs; F3 20261023-075225) and its posts from the screen's top as the start menu's, over its rows and slots, which reach the picture's edges), `VIWndMainMenu` with its seven bars, the character's bar, the stats column with `UpdateLocation`'s zone and ninth and the effects, sub-menus over it, `VIKeyFlash`'s repeat; its menus are CLIENT's data, read with gWorldInfo out of the decrypted image (`run/client.bin`; in a page, unpacked off the disc by `dnas`) through its relocations; entries go to `DoAction` or the mode-13 path; Quit asks with the message box, then logs out as `Process` does: 0x09B0 flushed at once, then `Disconnect(9, 1)`, a page's login screen saying "Logged out."). The group's bars (`DrawGroup`, from 0x0772's roster and state channel 0x40) and `VIWndMBox`, the Yes/No box an invitation asks with. The spell book (`VIWndSpellBook`, the start menu's Ability: level tabs, six ability plates (`BlitAbilityIcon`), the selected one's stats and description, the slots, five or ten; its popup Memorize/Arrange/Unmemorize All as `Send0033`/`Send003A`/`Send00FE`, answered by 0x00B4/0x00B6/0x00FF; 0x00FD sets an ability usable). The who list (`VIWndWhoList` over `VIClientPart38334`: 0x0E01/0x0E02 with a serial, ten rows of name, race, class and level, "searching..." while the reply is out; `VIWndOptionList`'s Tell, through the edit box as `/t`, and Invite, `InviteToGroup` with the row's id; the client's own sort, F3 20261023-085524/-090229: by name from the start, ○ (C) or a click on a heading for race, class or level, the selected row kept, Tell and Invite to the row shown, where the game keeps the server's order; on a server whose patch sorts the list itself, Sandstorm's S2, its sort instead (`wholist::PatchSort`, F3 20261023-185531): L1 and R1 or left and right go round the columns, the selection keeping its place in the list, the heading sorted by between the patch's marks and the patch's legend, its ranks, marks, places and words read out of the patch). `VIWndEdit`, the edit box its entries open (the soft keyboard, or the compact legend for a keyboard, the default here: `EQOA_SOFT_KEYBOARD=1`): Message, buddies, ignore, report. The spell book (`VIWndSpellBook`, the start menu's Ability: level tabs, six ability plates (`BlitAbilityIcon`), the selected one's stats and description, the slots, five or ten; its popup Memorize/Arrange/Unmemorize All as `Send0033`/`Send003A`/`Send00FE`, answered by 0x00B4/0x00B6/0x00FF; 0x00FD sets an ability usable). The inventory (the start menu's "Inventory": `VIWndItemMenu` over `VIWndList`/`VIWndMenu`/`VIWndMiniMenu`, the four tabs `UpdateQuickBars` rebuilds each frame with `VIInfoText`'s predicates, each item's sub-menu, the selected item's description with `StatWithItem`'s "Str: 5 -> 7", `VIWndInspect`, `VIWndQuantity`, the destroy prompt (for every item, as SOE's final live client asks: a worn, quick-slotted or NO TRADE one with words 916-918, as the disc's, and an ordinary one with the live 1841, 'Destroy item "<name>"?', where the disc's destroyed it at once; `VIWndItemMenu::Use`, live 0x013738E0), the quick-slot mode; item icons from ITEMICON.ESF, "?" where one is missing; the inventory kept by 0x003B-0x0040, 0x000A and 0x0060 with `VIItemList`'s rules; use/equip 0x003E, destroy 0x003C, arrange 0x003D, prepare 0x000A, and the HUD's quick item uses it too). The NPC and player windows: the corpse (`VIWndLoot`, mode 1: 0x0018/0x0016, take one 0x0019, take all 0x001A with `Msg001A`), the merchant (`VIWndMerchant`/`VIWndMercMenu`, mode 10: 0x00B7, buy 0x004A, sell 0x004B with its worn/quick-slotted/no-trade prompt and, as SOE's final live client (`Sell`, live 0x013858A0), 1842's 'Sell item "<name>"?' for anything but a misc item (loot sells at once), price, coin and `StatWithItem` lines; in a window wider than 4:3 it stays the game's 4:3 picture as the start menu does, the HUD beside it, a post closing each of its edges from the top rail down to the bottom one, the client's (until 2026-10-23 its left panel, rails and right frames ran to the window's sides)), and trading (`VIWndTrade`/`VIWndTradeList`/`VIWndDeposit`, modes 15/16: 0x0057 and 0x0056's sub-opcodes, `TradeA`/`TradeB`'s numbering, the accept glow, money, chat line); `VIWndQuantity` with its total. Live on a lane server with go-eqoa-server's loot/trade handlers. Mail and the tell forms (`gram.rs`, `mail.rs`): `VIClientPart2EFD0`, the gram client (0x0D01 requests with the inner 0x0D41 as a blob, 0x0D41 replies gated by its state; unit tests on the bytes and the gate), `VIWndComposeGram` (Mail > Compose, mode 23; the reader's and the buddy list's reply copies), `VIWndGramInbox` and `VIWndReadGram` (Mail > Inbox, mode 24: the list, the text, Reply with "Re: ", Delete), `VIWndTell` and `VIWndReply` (modes 28/29, "/t name text" to `ChatLine`); `EQOA_MAIL_TEXT=a|b|c` types the fields in a playshot. Live against a lane server with go-eqoa-server's mail handler: compose, the inbox, read, reply, delete and the unknown-recipient box. SOE's final live client's mail (read off Corsten's retail state, `c:/eqoa-live-1500`), after a server's offer (go-eqoa-server's 0x7E0A, answered 1; `Grams::live`): sub-ops 1 and 3 with no envelope, a send to every name `ParseRecipients` finds (letters, ',' or ';' between, capitalised; the To 512 long), its answers 9 (the unknown names in the box, the live 890 and ": ") and 4 (the names not delivered to, red, one line a reason, then the window closes), 839 said by the window, the To line under From in the reader and its Reply All (the sender in the player's own place, `JoinNames` with ';'); unit tests on the bytes, each answer, the windows and Reply All, with the disc's forms as the controls. The inbox's Next and Prev go round, as the live client's, on every server. |
| the quick chat | `quickchat.rs`: L2 in play (the keyboard's Z) opens `VIWndMenu4` (CLIENT, mode 30) over the MemoryDump's quick-chat sets (`LoadTextSets` into `vi::textset`, every dump; the server keeps them): four bars at (175, 18) with the d-pad's arrows, each choice's label (`%W<n>%` its word) with a branch's mark or an empty choice's; the HUD's quick menus stay up under it (L2 opens it without `UpdateMode`) and the effects' bar goes. A tap runs the choice: a line is said (`VIClientText::Expand`'s `%1`..`%4`: the name, level, class and target), `|<n>` is quick command n of 34 (`DoAction`: the four emotes, invite, boot, leave, ignore the target (the edit box with its name), the privacy list, shouts and the guild's chat heard or not (0x0C01's flag, the guild's; Ignore Shouts is the client's to keep, `settings::shouts`, `shouts=off` in `client.opt`, each window its own: the game keeps none and a server forgets it at the next login, so the client tells it again once a login, `Play::tell_shouts`, and drops another player's shout that still comes, `chat::is_shout`: 0x0A7B's (63, 255, 63) and "<name> shouts: "; the player's own shout is kept; the user's, 2026-10-25), say, group, shout and guild, reply, tell, the chat line, the two assists, roll, the pet's seven, follow, the time (`VITimeOfDay::GetLocalTime`, the machine's clock, its weekday as shipped: a leap February is a day out) and the alarm (`VIAlarm`: an edit box for its H:MM, a message box when it rings; `/alarm` and `/time` in the chat line too); Stop Ignoring has no case in `DoAction` and does nothing, as in the game), then the menus close; a branch opens its set in a menu 16 pixels on over this one, which dims to 0.3 (each lights from nothing when it gets the keys), three deep. Held for 16 of the game's frames (about half a second at any display rate) a direction opens `VIWndMenu4Edit` on its choice: the name in its own `VIWndEdit` at (32, 190), the line as a text or one of the 34 commands from a list that glides a row in six frames (the EE's adder: five steps of 0.2 make 0.99999994), a line made a branch or a branch a line, Done or back; Done sends 0x006C (the choice), 0x006A (a new branch's empty set) and 0x006B (each set a branch made a line held); "Def. Menu4" (0x0B080000) puts back the built-in tree (`DefaultTextSets`, its commands numbered as shipped), sent. △, L2, Escape and Z close the menus, Enter and R3 open the chat line in their place, and every other key passes from the window with the keys down the chain to the game window as the game passes it (✕ let go stops the attack). On a pointer a bar is its direction tapped, a mouse press holds it (half a second: the editor), and the editor's rows and command list take clicks; the touch screen's pad keeps its d-pad over a menu. Here and not in the game: a set the dump did not give reads as an empty one (the game reads through a null pointer), and a choice with both a line and a branch runs the line and closes (the game then leaves its hidden branch with the keys). `EQOA_QUICK_TEXT=a\|b` types into the editor's edit box in a playshot | `vi::textset` against CLIENT's own (above). Unit tests: a tap's line and command (and a number past 33 doing nothing), a branch dimming the root and lighting itself, keys passed up (△ closing, L3's release going nowhere), the hold's 16 frames at 30 Hz and on `frames.rs`'s clock at 165 Hz, the editor's rename through its edit box and command pick with the 0x006C it sends, a branch made (0x006C, 0x006A) and dropped (0x006B for its tree), cancel, the list's glide in the EE's six frames (the host's five as the control), the defaults (19 0x006A and the root's four 0x006C), sscanf's `|%d`, a pointer on the bars, the editor's rows and the list (each failing without its hit notes), the alarm and the weekday; the touch pad's d-pad over the menus with another window as the control. Live on the local go-eqoa-server through `EQOA_PAD`: its root (eqoa.live's 25 default sets) drawn, a branch over the dimmed root, "Yes" said and heard back, Wave run and the menus closed, a held branch's "Branch Options", the command list at Cheer, a rename sent as 0x006C (set 1, slot 2, the line and the new label, which its box cut to "I don't ..."); go-eqoa-server does not keep 0x006A/B/C yet, so a change lasts until the next dump |
| the updater | `update.rs`, the downloaded builds only: the dev release's `update.txt` (dev.yml's: `version`, `commit`, a `build <platform> <file> <sha256> <size>` line for linux-x86_64, windows-x86_64 and macos), read on a thread of its own as the game starts; another commit's program downloaded to `<exe>.new` (ureq over rustls), held to its size and SHA-256, put in place by two renames (the running one aside to `<exe>.old`, or the first `<exe>.old-<n>` free while that one cannot be removed: Windows removes and replaces no running program, and another window may still run the one the last update set aside, F3 20261027-054237; a read-only one made writable first and the read-only flag never carried to the new program; every one removed that can be at the start; the old one back when the second fails), `<exe>.lock` keeping a second window of the game out of it; the boot waits for it at its last loading picture (30 s at most, a line on the picture while it downloads), then closes the window and `exec`s (Windows: starts) the new program with the same arguments and `EQOA_UPDATED`, which does not ask again as it starts. While the game runs (`watch`, the program `EQOA_UPDATED` started too) the same thread looks again 3 minutes after the start and every 10 after: another commit's program put in place, or found there (another window's, the start's download that came after the title; not one with this program's own bytes), is offered with what is new in it: the release's `update-notes.md` (dev.yml uploads UPDATE-NOTES.md under that name before `update.txt`, notes.yml again for an edit to the notes alone), read by the same thread, set against the build's own UPDATE-NOTES.md (`include_str!`): the sections it lacks, newest first (`whats_new`; none new, or none read, is a line naming the release page). The question is the game's titled window (`updatewnd.rs`, `VIUIWindow` set 0 as `VIWndMessage`): the sections' titles and bullets in a framed list with its scroll bar (`DrawFrameVScroll`), the pad's ✕ △ ○ □ as the UI's inline glyphs, then Restart now / Later, starting on Later; up once `Play::quiet`/`Pregame::quiet` have held two seconds (the controls let go, sticks included), taking only its own keys (Enter, Escape, the cursor keys, Page Up/Down, Home, End; the pad's ✕, △ and d-pad), the wheel, a stick (the walk's sticks and the look are its while it is up), a drag on the list and a click on a choice; left alone three minutes it is a Later. Restart now logs out as Quit does (0x09B0) and the window closes for the same relaunch, Later asks again after 30 minutes (`EQOA_UPDATE_EVERY=<s>` shortens all three for tests). The channel and commit are baked in by CI (`EQOA_UPDATE_CHANNEL`, `EQOA_BUILD_COMMIT`; dev.yml refuses a program that does not carry its commit); `EQOA_NO_UPDATE`, `EQOA_OFFSCREEN` and the scripted commands leave it out, `EQOA_UPDATE_URL=<base>` reads a server of one's own instead (offscreen too) | unit tests over a server of the test's own: the manifest's lines, another commit put in place (the old aside, nothing left over), this commit, the same bytes under another commit and no answer leaving it, a download of another hash or size or none leaving the program as it was, a second window leaving it to the first, the rollback, the boot's wait, a program aside that cannot be removed (a directory in its place: another name taken, and taken again at the next update) and the start's clean-up finding every one by its name; three planted bugs each fail one. End to end on this machine: a build of one commit, started, downloaded a build of another off `python3 -m http.server`, put it in its place and `exec`ed it, which said so and removed the old one; started again, it asked once and stayed. The question while the game runs: unit tests (the watch offering another program put in place or found there, not this commit's nor one with this program's own bytes; the box up only with a program ready and the moment quiet, starting on No, a No keeping it down for its while, a window of the game's putting it away unanswered, left standing a No, a click on its buttons, only its own keys; the pregame's quiet screens), four planted bugs each failing one; `web/pagetest.py`'s Later coming back after its while. End to end, two release builds of different commits and `python3 -m http.server`: at the title the box came up once the served `update.txt` moved on, ✕ on No put it away and left the title's menu as it was, it came back after its while, and Yes started the other build (the old one gone at its start); in the world, against a go-eqoa-server built from its head, Yes logged out (the server's `Logout: … (0x09B0)`) and the new build logged straight back in. What is new in it: unit tests (the notes' sections, bullets joined and markdown out; the sections a build lacks, several when builds were skipped, none, an empty file; the built-in notes reading newest first; the notes fetched beside the manifest, a release without them an error; the window's wrapping, an address broken after a '/'; its list's lines, titles and the line naming the release page; keys, a page, Home and End, a stick and the wheel kept inside the list; Later first; a press on a choice and a drag on the list), five planted bugs each failing one; `web/pagetest.py`'s notice saying what is new (and not when nothing is), a planted bug failing it; dev.yml's publish and notes.yml's step run against a stand-in `gh` (update-notes.md uploaded with the programs before update.txt and kept by the old-asset sweep; again after an edit to the notes alone; nothing when the program changed too). End to end, served notes with two sections newer than the build's: at the title the window listed both (the pad's glyphs inline, the markdown out), a stick scrolled it, ✕ on Later put it away and left the title's menu as it was, it came back, and Restart now started the other build; in the world, the d-pad scrolled it, Restart now logged out (the server's `Logout`) and the new build logged straight back in; a release without the notes showed the line naming the release page and still offered the restart; in a real window on the Xvfb display, the wheel (xdotool) scrolled the list and a drag moved it with the pointer |
| the log | `logfile.rs` natively: stderr through a pipe (`dup2`ed over fd 2 on Unix and `SetStdHandle` on Windows), a thread handing each line on to `eqoa.log` and to where stderr went, through a thread of its own (a launcher's pipe that nothing reads stops no frame); a window past the first, `multibox`'s place, keeps `eqoa-<n>.log`; at the close stderr is put back and the pipe read to its end before the log's last line, `[eqoa] closed` (a run ended by an error, a disc image not chosen, closes its log in `main` too); a panic's message and backtrace into the log, its message straight to the terminal, as the pipe's reader is a thread a panic does not wait for; `caught` steps over a library's own fault (gilrs's lookup of a controller it holds no state for yet). A graphics device lost natively (`gpulost.rs`; F3 crash 20261024-001640, a Windows PC let sleep with the game running: wgpu then refuses every buffer, and `create_buffer_init` panics on the first) closes the window at the next event and starts the game again in its place, as the update's restart does; the panic of an upload in the frame that lost it starts it again from the hook, its log closed; a device lost within 30 s of a start ends the run, its log saying why (no loop). `EQOA_GPU_LOST_TEST=<ms>[,frame]` destroys the device then, `frame` uploading after it. In a page `web/notices.js` says what goes wrong: a part gone is a notice to dismiss (`web::problem`), the client stopped one to reload by (`web::crash`) | unit tests (a caught panic comes back as its message, nested too, the same panic outside `caught` the control; a terminal that reads nothing stops no line of the log, the stuck writer the control); `web/pagetest.py` (the client stopped is one notice, with Reload); `EQOA_GPU_LOST_TEST` offscreen (`eqoa boot`): the device lost between two events, and lost before an upload (the crash's own panic, 'vertices' label is invalid), each closed its log and started the game again; lost 8 s after the start, no start again |
| memory | `mem.rs`: what the log says of the memory (F3 crash 20261026-220031: a Windows client on an Intel UHD under DX12 stopped after 11 hours at Freeport's busiest spot with Rust's "memory allocation of 229376 bytes failed", the size of 2,048 of the HUD's 112-byte quads (its list outgrowing 1,024: the last straw, no leak's own allocation); nothing it sent could tell a leak from a machine short of memory). Taken every 10 s on a frame that draws (`tick`; the allocator's report every minute, it lists every allocation): the process's resident memory and its peak and what it holds as its own (Windows' `K32GetProcessMemoryInfo`, the working set and the commit charge, what an allocation fails on, each with its peak; Linux's `/proc/self/status`, VmRSS, VmHWM, RssAnon and VmSwap; a Mac's `proc_pid_rusage`, resident, footprint and the footprint's peak), the machine's (`GlobalMemoryStatusEx`'s free memory and commit charge and limit, `/proc/meminfo`'s, a Mac's `hw.memsize` and memory pressure level), wgpu's counts (its `counters` feature: buffers and bind groups, the bytes under buffers and textures; wgpu-hal 30's Vulkan counts no textures made) and, under Vulkan and DX12, its allocator's blocks, the biggest three and the five labels holding most (on an integrated GPU these are the machine's memory too); a page's wasm heap and Chromium's JavaScript heap. The log has a `[memory]` line every 10 minutes (`EQOA_MEM_EVERY=<s>` for a test's run) | unit tests (sizes, /proc's lines with a longer key as the control, this process measured and an empty sample as the control, the allocator summed by label and a report from before saying when, the log's line once a period, the samples between kept as the last the control); an hour's soak offscreen at Freeport's busy spot on go-eqoa-server (1920x1009 at 60 frames a second, Vulkan on an RX 580; three bot clients in and out every 1-4 minutes, 69 logins each a new entity, 1,660 chat lines, a spell visual every 20 s, a teleport round four spots each minute): the process's own memory 287 MB from the first minute to the last, the allocator 512 MB reserved and 159-166 MB used all hour, its buffers (1,900-2,430) and bind groups (1,570-1,740) rising and falling with what was in view, the texture cache settling at 315 textures, and the GPU driver's own figures (amdgpu's fdinfo: 520 MB of VRAM, 16 MB of GTT) flat as well; a traveller (a teleport every 15 s to 61 places across Tunaria) went from 278 MB to 1.1 GB, about 14 MB a place, its textures from 55 to 320 MB, and kept all of it standing at Freeport after: no zone is let go (`stream.rs` drops a zone's GPU group past the outer radius; the library and the collision world keep it, where `VIWorld::StreamLeaf` releases it, `ReleaseStatic`, `RemoveActorsFromZone`, `ResetToProxy`), the texture cache neither; at most the whole of Tunaria (1,980 MB, its 1,485 distinct textures 233 MB) |
| the options | The start menu's Options (`VIWndOptions`, mode 34, `options.rs`; on a Mac also the app menu's Settings… with ⌘,, the system's own place for them, `macos.rs`: an item added to the menu winit makes, its action setting a flag the frame takes, opening the Options window in the world (not over another window) and the title's Options in the pregame (when the title's menu has the keys); ⌘, as a key too): Game (Faction Messages, Loot Messages, Damage Numbers), Interface (Auto Attack, 3rd Person Camera, Camera Pitch Axis, Soft Keyboard, Vibration), Sound (Battle Music, Fx and Music Volume, Sound Output) and Save Changes (asked with the Yes/No box, "Saving..." four frames); the camera, pitch, volumes, output, music and keyboard change as they are moved, the messages, Auto Attack and Vibration only once saved, and Triangle on the menu puts the moved ones back. Kept as the game keeps `VIGameOptions` (`settings.rs`): `game.opt`, the game's 1,024 bytes (vi.h's layout, little-endian, the magic at +0x3FC) with the login Station's Save Account keeps (+0x10..+0x90) and the server list's home shard (+0x98), read at each login before the options are used (the two sound classes' volumes set from it as `VIWndInitFromMC` sets them; not the output mode, which the console does not set either) and written by Save Changes (`SaveOptions`); natively in `$EQOA_CONFIG`, else `eqoa-rs` in the config directory (`~/.config/eqoa-rs`, `~/Library/Application Support/eqoa-rs` on a Mac, `%APPDATA%\eqoa-rs` on Windows) for `eqoa play` (a `playshot` or `shot` keeps and reads nothing without `$EQOA_CONFIG`), in a page the origin's localStorage (`eqoa.game.opt`), which may refuse (a private window: the defaults, nothing kept). Each window of several keeps its own options by its place (`multibox`; F3 20261023-082517, the user's choice): window 1 in `game.opt` and `client.opt` as ever, window n in `game-<n>.opt` and `client-<n>.opt` (a page's `eqoa.game-<n>.opt`, `eqoa.client-<n>.opt`), so one window plays with sound and another without; a window that has kept none yet starts from window 1's options and home shard, not its saved account (two windows cannot play one account); Controller Focus and Capture Controller stay in `client.opt`, which a later window's save updates, window 1's own lines left as they are; the keys and buttons (`keys.opt`) are the computer's, and a save writes them only when its Controls page changed them, so a window saving its sound never puts back keys another window changed since. The client's own **Extended**, between Sound and Save Changes (its words the client's, the menu a row longer; the user's, 2026-10-25: "our options, extended is getting hard to parse for folks, lets make it Extended, then Video, UI, Controller, etc."): a menu of its own in the options menu's window and place, titled Extended (the menu not drawn under it, whose words would show through), of Video, World, UI, Controller, Camera, Characters and Sound, each opening a page laid out as the game's pages are (a row down, the legend two under the last; no page more than six rows, so none scrolls, and a seventh row is a compile error, `options::PAGE_MOST`); Triangle goes back a step (a page to the categories, its category still lit; the categories to the options menu). Kept beside the game's in `client.opt` and put back by Triangle as they are. **Video**: Display (natively: Window or Full Screen, borderless on the window's monitor: the window's own state, which F11 and Alt+Enter toggle too, Alt+Enter's Enter never reaching the game, and the row shows after either; it changes at once and stays, kept with the window's place, `winplace`, and Triangle leaves it; `display.rs`; not on a page, whose full screen is the browser's F11; F3 20261024-201507, "Cant get the client out of windowed mode", the user's, 2026-10-25), Brightness (a slider: the scene through a curve, `(level/255)^(2^((5 - step)/5))`, the HUD left as drawn; the middle step no pass at all), Frame Rate (30, 60 or the display's: until chosen, a page on WebGL2 keeps 30, `?uncapped` the display's, and everything else the display's; natively the window sleeps until a capped frame is due), VSync (natively On, the default, shows a frame at the display's refresh, `PresentMode::Fifo`, where AutoVsync's FifoRelaxed tore on a late frame, F3 20261024-065802; Off the first of Immediate, Mailbox, Fifo the surface offers, the loop capped at the monitor's refresh; `vsync=off` in `client.opt`, each window's own; inert in a page, where the browser paces), Loading Screens (Longer, the default: a trip of 200 or more that has a PS2 read zones off the disc (`loading::needs_read`: one within 900 of the arrival that was not within 900 of where it stood; none for Sandstorm's boon, all of Freeport moved into its sky at once, where a held walk stood in the air a second with nothing sent and the players moved after it were put on it), or into another world, a 0x00B2's or a dump's, keeps the game's own loading screen up at least a second, `loading::FAR_HOLD_MS`, as a PS2 shows it while `EnterWorld` reads the zones round a far arrival off the disc, where this client has them in about a millisecond and Return Home jumped from one place to the other, F3 20261025-171303; Instant: down as soon as the place is in; `loading=instant` in `client.opt` only when Instant; the user's, 2026-10-25: "so if players want the instant or the longer loading screens") and Text (Sharp, the default: the game's font with even strokes at any window size, `render::ui::TextFilter`: a texel's own colour across it and the next one's mixed only over a band round its edge, a whole pixel from two pixels a texel and narrowing to none as a texel nears one pixel, where the nearest texel had made some of the font's two-texel strokes a pixel wider than others at the fractional scale of almost every window; | the map | M (the client's own: the game has none; F3 20261021, jib), full screen over the game (`map/`): the world the player is in, drawn from the player's disc, zone by zone. A zone is a cell of the world's 2,000-unit grid (so it is in all six worlds; Lavastorm's first is x 4357..6000 in its cell) and its record holds every palette and texture its terrain names, so it is read alone (the streaming's `ZoneSource`; natively on a thread), its terrain built a few patches at a time (`esf::Container::build_terrain`) and its placed actors one sprite at a time (as the scene places them, their colours their static light; animated HSprites left out), drawn from straight above by a `Renderer` of the map's own with the light pinned from the north-west and no fog into a 512-texel tile (3.9 world units a texel), read back (`render::map::TileShot`) and let go with its textures: no more than a zone is held. A piece of that a step (patches, a sprite, a texture decoded, an upload, the picture, its cell, a few rows of its PNG), 2 ms a frame behind the game from 5 s after a world is entered and 6 ms while the map is up (at most 12% and 35% of a frame, so a 165 Hz display keeps most of its rate), the zones the walk has read first, then nearest the player (or the map's middle while it is up). Kept as PNGs (the tile and its 128-texel overview cell) under a key of the world's records, so a world is drawn once on a machine: natively `eqoa-rs/map/<key>` in the cache directory (`$EQOA_MAP_CACHE`), in a page the origin's private storage (`main.js`'s `eqoaMap*`). Drawn (`render::map::MapLayer`, trilinear, mips made here): the overview (one texture a world, its mips whole per cell), the tiles the view wants (40 at most on the GPU, from the cache), a cell not drawn yet plain, the grid; the cells' names, the game's (`ZoneName`'s grid by `UpdateLocation`'s cell within `gWorldInfo`'s bounds, WORDS.TXT's words; CLIENT read at run time); the title the start menu's location line with the world's name (`gWorldInfo`'s word at +0x08: "Tunaria: Freeport (SE)"); the group's other members in the compass's colours; the player a dart pointing its heading's way, north up as the compass has it, both the HUD's size at every zoom (the dart 7 of the frame's 448 lines to its tip, a member's dot 2.5: `view::marker_radius`; F3 20261023, Liav's: 0.022 of the window's height and 6 pixels covered a zone's square zoomed out). M (free in `VIWndGame::OnKeyDown`'s table; not while an edit box has the keys) or a click or tap on the HUD's compass (the touch screen's way) opens it on the player's area, 2,500 units across the shorter side, following the player; Escape, M, Backspace, △ (Start), a right click or Close close it, as does a window coming up or a death. The wheel (about the pointer, which stops the following), the right stick, + and - (L1 and R1 a step, L2 and R2 held) and a pinch zoom from a world unit a pixel out to the whole world; a drag, the left stick, the arrows, WASD and the d-pad pan, by the clock; ✕, Space or Home go back to the player. Its buttons and the pad legend's glyphs take a pointer (`Map::point`). No pad combination opens it yet (jib's "TBD"). `eqoa mapshot <world> <out.png> [x z heading scale secs]` builds and draws it offline; `EQOA_MAP=ms` opens it in a playshot; `EQOA_MAP_TRACE=1` (a page's `?maptrace`) prints any build step over 2 ms | Unit tests, each with its control: north up and east right, to the window and back, a zoom keeping the point under the pointer, the limits (a unit a pixel; the whole of Tunaria with room), the marker's heading (the compass's convention), the markers' size (the frame's lines, not the zoom; the old size the control), a held pan the same at 30 and 165 frames a second, the sticks, the mouse's drag, click and wheel (about the pointer, the following given up), a pinch following the fingers, the keyboard (an M's repeat does not close what it opened), the tiles a view wants, the cells (Tunaria's, Lavastorm's), the names (`ZoneName` a row and column back), the ninth, the cache key, the cache's listing (a tile kept with its cell), the nearest zone first, the tile drawn north up from straight above, a zone made a piece a frame (Freeport's: hundreds of frames with no time to spare, one with), PNGs whole and a few rows at a time, the buttons through `pointer::testing`'s raster, the compass. `esf/tests/terrain_alone.rs`: zones of all six worlds built from their records alone equal the library's terrain, piece by piece too, every texture in the record. Live on the local go-eqoa-server: Freeport (a 3D shot from straight above against the map at a unit a pixel: the walls, the coast and the piers line up), Tunaria's north-west and south-east corners against `#pos` (z past 32,000 is the 0x40's own clamp, CLIENT's `[-4000, 32000]`), Lavastorm (`#goto 4768 100 4500 3`: rebuilt for its two zones, "Lavastorm Mountains: Solusek's Eye"). Measured: natively Tunaria in 25 s with the map up (6.2 s of work, 121 MB peak); in Chromium on WebGPU 36-43 s (10-11 s of work, no step over 5 ms) at 60 fps, the wasm heap 278 MB through it (266 on a visit with it kept), the next visit's 350 files read back in 0.5 s; on WebGL2 59 s at 30 fps, the heap unchanged Its markers (`map/markers.rs`, the user's F3 pass 18 asks): EQOA-Data's 49 XP camps (github.com/Jadiction/EQOA-Data, MIT) at go-eqoa-server's places for them, every coachman where it spawns, the coach map's routes and EQOA-Data's 36 towns (Tunaria's where the server's coach menus put them, Odus's by a fit of the camps' points, Oggok in Rathe: Frontiers' Tunaria has no zone where the guide's sheet puts it) in `map/markers_data.rs`, written by `tools/gen_map_markers.py`; a coach wears the stable sign's horseshoe (surface 0x9BE57886, on Freeport's sign by Coachman Ronks, in no file but the world's zones) read off the player's disc at run time (a zone in play, one the map reads, or that zone peeked once) and kept in the map's cache, a drawn horseshoe meanwhile; a card under the mouse, a finger's tap (a second tap, or a click, puts the waypoint on it) and the pad's middle cross; routes dashed (the pointed coach's whole and bright), town names on a plate by side, placed biggest first clear of the arrow, the chrome and the map's own names. Tested: the markers in their worlds, the cards' text, a hit test with a control, the tap/click/toggle routes, the horseshoe kept in the cache and found in a zone's records, the routes once each to their stops, dashes cut to the window, names giving way, the chrome's footprint. The community's markers (`map/community.rs`, `mapsuggest.rs`; the user's 2026-10-26 "Players can submit waypoints to be added to the in-game map. Submissions would require official approval, similar to our spellfx", one list on eqoaclassic for every player on any server, suggested from the map): `GET <base>mapmarks` (`BASE` eqoaclassic's account server through its web server, `EQOA_MAPMARKS=<base>` another; none offscreen, and `mapshot` only with it) when the map first opens in a run and every 10 minutes while it is up, natively on a thread with the last ETag (`If-None-Match`; a 304 keeps the list), in a page the browser's fetch with `cache: "no-cache"` (it revalidates with its own); the list kept in the map's cache (`store`, "community/marks.json", its ETag in it) for a start offline; a list that does not read, or an error, keeps the one held, said once in the log. A pin each (`community::draw_pins`: a head of the kind's colour on a point to the spot, over the camps), its name in close (`NAMES_FROM`) placed as the towns' are and clear of them, a card under the mouse, a tap (a second tap, or a click, puts the waypoint on it) and the pad's cross, over a camp's or coach's card; X hides them with the markers. L3 (N, the Suggest button, beside the others) asks for the spot (this world's waypoint, else the pointer's place but not the button's own, else the pad's cross once panned, else the middle; `Map::take_suggest`); Play puts the map away for the game's edit box (purposes `0x7E30_0001`/`0x7E30_0002`): the name (2-40 letters, else refused here, nothing sent), then a note (up to 200, an empty Enter none); `POST <base>mapmarks/suggest` `{world, x, z, name, note, client}` on a thread (a page's fetch), the map back where it was, under its title "Sending the marker...", then "Sent for a GM's approval." or the list's own sentence for six seconds; Escape at either step sends nothing. Tested, each with its control and its planted bug failing it: the list's body (a mark with no place or name passed over, an unknown kind `Other`, no list or no JSON none), a suggestion's body (cut by characters) and its answers (the server's sentence, a 200 refusal, a gateway's page), asked only while the map is up, kept, asked again with the ETag and kept on a 304, the cache's list on a start with no server (none kept, none shown), a bad list and a 500 keeping the held one, the pins under the pointer (another world's never, a tap then the waypoint, the pad's cross, hidden by X), the spot's order (the button's pointer not a spot), the notice under the title, and the whole suggestion through the edit box's own keys against a stand-in server (Enter's body, Escape at either step, a one-letter name, the list's refusal shown, an empty note sent). The target and the group's waypoint (F3 20261026-231048, Liav's, and -212034; `map/group_waypoint.rs`, `groupmark.rs`): while the map follows the player □ goes on the target in view (`Context::target`, ringed white; once the map is panned its middle wins), and Suggest takes the spot chosen last (this visit's waypoint, the pointer or the pad's cross, the target with its name typed in the box, an older waypoint, the middle); □ held 0.6 s by the clock (Q held), O (binds' `map_group_waypoint`; G till the attack took it), Shift and a click (the viewer's `ModifiersChanged`) or a finger held still (`TouchPad::take_holds`) asks Play to say the spot in the group's chat through `/g` (0x000E 2, 0x000B, 0x000E back), one every 5 s, as "Waypoint in <world> at x <n>, z <n>[ near <area>]" (the map's world and cell names, WORDS.TXT's); 0x069E is its own `Event::GroupChat`, and a line there in exactly those words from a member of the roster (the player's own too) puts the group's waypoint, orange on the map (its sender's name and distance under it), the minimap's rim and the compass's, one at a time, gone when reached after being away, when its sender or the player leaves the group, or with a click on it (Sandstorm words its group lines the same, its players' F3 chat logs show). Tested, each with its control and two planted bugs failing them: the line's round trip and every other wording refused (a say, a shout, a tell, a fraction, a sign, seven digits, more before or after), names in any case, the event of its own (0x0A7A's same words a plain line), the target's spot following and the middle panned, □ held the same in seconds at 30 and 165 Hz (a repeat and the release nothing, a close forgetting it), Shift's click and a finger's hold (a coach's own spot, not on a button), reached only after being away, gone with its sender and the group, the spot Suggest takes in each case, the world named both ways, the legend's words only in a group, and Play's whole round: G in a group says the line in the group's channel with the mode put back (out of a group nothing, a second within 5 s nothing), a member's line puts it and a non-member's, a say's, an unnamed world's and other words do not. |

| the GM's channel | `VIClientPart382E8` (CLIENT `viadmin.cpp`; `admin.rs`, `adminplay.rs`): 0x0700 turns it on (sub-op 35, a zigzag int: above 0 the commands, not 0 the menu entry) or asks a Yes/No question with a name (50: word 1189, Yes 0x0701 48, No 49). On, the start menu keeps its first entry, TEST (`OpenStartMenu`): Avatar (the target's actions, Buff PC, Broadcast, Render), Navigation (Goto Zone, Goto Location, Goto Player, Run Speed), Design, Programming (Show BPS, Trigger, Network, Server Load) and Version. Every command is one 0x0701 (a sub-op and its fields as `Show`, `DoText`, `Send16`/`Send17`/`Send33`/`Toggle19` write them) or 0x0702 (`Do2`); a command that asks first opens the game window's edit box (`ShowNameClear`, `ShowTargetPlayerName`); `/create`, `/mastery`, `/tradelvl` while on, and SOE's final live client's `/rst_mastr`, `/rst_both`, `/rem_abil` (0x0701 57-59 and the text), `/banktunar` (56 and a zigzag number) and `/servdown` (101); the auction's hours in minutes for a GM (`InMinutes`). The TEST menu's own entries act on what the client has: the creatures' walk and glide (Extrapolate, Interpolate), the hour held (day-night), the personal light, the weather, Flora, Hide UI, Fast run, Show BPS, DISPLAY POS, Toggle Stats. Windows: `VIWndBuffPC` (mode 27, `buffpc.rs`), `VIWndGoto` (mode 4, `gotownd.rs`: `MoveToGrid`'s square, `MoveTo`'s floor cast from 300 above to 300 below, again once the zones there are in, `Placing::MoveTo`, as `EnterWorld` streams them before it casts), `VIWndNetStat` and `DrawNetStats` (`netstat.rs`, the server-load channels 0x44-0x6B). The network simulation's values (sub-op 18) are sent and set on the route; the socket-level lag-and-loss simulator under them is not in `drdp`. go-eqoa-server sends it to a GM's account since 2026-10-28 (`handlers/gmchannel.go`). Not retail: a server that names its triggers offers the names beside the channel (0x7E07, a u32, answered 2 by `net::session`, `TRIGGERS_ALL`, as a PS2 ignores it; a server from before 2026-11-09 takes any answer but 0 as 1) and sends them, the whole set each time they change (0x7E08: a u8 count, each trigger's u8 line 0-7, u8 flags, bit 0 on and bit 1 running, and a name of at most 32 bytes of UTF-8; `admin::read_triggers`); Programming's TRIGGER lines then show the names (`Menus::labels`, keyed by `Do2`'s message and the entry's id, a `Label`), one switched off drawn in a dim grey (a greyed teal selected: `Label::dim`), " *" after one running, a name too wide for its bar cut with "..." before that tail (`startmenu::fit_label`), an unnamed line the game's word. A server that lists every trigger sends the whole table in 0x7E08's place for the answer 2 (0x7E36: a u16 count, each trigger's u32 slot, u8 flags and name; `admin::read_trigger_list`), and the TRIGGER menu is then a line a trigger that is on, by slot (`adminplay::set_trigger_list`: the client's own message `MSG_TRIGGER_SLOT` with the slot for its id, labelled "slot name", an unnamed one's slot and the game's word; CLIENT's eight lines kept in `Menus::trigger_lines` and put back with no list or none on), seven bars shown and scrolled as `VIWndMainMenu` scrolls any longer menu, an open menu's selection kept within its lines when the list changes (`MainMenu::refit`); a line fires its own slot (0x7E37, a u32, `admin::fire_slot`), where `Do2`'s byte reaches the first 256 | every 0x0701 sub-op's bytes against the decomp's writes, `Recv0700`, the start menu with and without TEST off CLIENT's image, the trigger names read and drawn on their bars (with no names, the game's word; one switched off in the dim colours, on in black and the selected teal) and their keys on the image's TRIGGER entries, the whole list read, made into the TRIGGER menu (on the image's TEST tree too, CLIENT's eight put back) and paged through `VIWndMainMenu`'s own keys, its lines firing 0x7E37, both windows through `pointer::testing`, the slash commands, the auction's minutes, the flow through `Play` over a recording session, each with a control. Live (2026-10-28) against go-eqoa-server with a test hook (0x0700 35 at world entry for an acctlevel 1 account, every 0x0701 logged and decoded): the TEST menu, Navigation and the Goto window, Goto's teleport, BROADCAST echoed, the prompt answered, KILL on the target, the simulation's values, TRIGGER, the position and BPS readouts |
| `snd` | The sound, as the PS2 makes it: the SPU2 (eqoa-port's `spu2.c`: ADPCM, Gaussian interpolation, ADSR, the two cores' mix), SNDDRV, the IOP's sound driver (`modules/snddrv`: the command handlers, the 100 Hz XM thread, the BGM stream, the XM player reading its module in place), with the IOP's threads as state machines on the SPU2's sample clock; and `VISoundDevice`, `VIWave`, `VIXm` (the EE half: playbacks and handles, priority stealing, the SPU and XM-pool allocators, uploads a block a frame, the BGM request slots and cross-fade), generic over the float model | **sample for sample against the decomp**: eqoa-port's `build/oracle_snd` runs the decomp's SNDDRV over the same SPU2 on a deterministic coroutine IOP, and the decomp's own VISoundDevice above it. `tests/oracle_snd.rs`: UI.CSF's waves, AMBTRACK.ESF's modules and two BGMs through every command, 1,924,000 frames and the status blocks equal; `tests/oracle_snddev.rs`: 2,233 calls as the game makes them, 729 packets equal to the byte, 1,132,000 frames equal. Planted samples, the ADPCM rounding, the XM pan law and the BGM fade step each fail them. Found: `VIWave::Create` sets volume 1 and pan 0 over the record's. Departures, each only where the console loses a command, each with its test, control and planted bug: nothing that finds the 128-word packet full is dropped, where the console's `SoundCmd` drops it (a crowd's frame: a pack's swings and hits started at once, 26 words a sound, and a 24-creature fight overflows the packet about one frame in ten): it and what comes after it wait in the device's backlog and go in the packets after the first, in order, at once while the IOP takes them (`tests/device_backlog.rs`, `tests/device_upload.rs`; with the console's own rule, `SoundDevice::console_drops`, `tests/oracle_snddev.rs`'s packets are the decomp's, its crowd's full frame included); an upload's next block waits for the last one's copy; and the client's own frames: between two of the game's frames a playing sound's volume, pan and pitch are held for the game's frame (`SoundDevice::set_between`, told by the viewer every frame drawn), so Frame Rate 60 and 165 send the console's packets word for word and make its sound sample for sample (`tests/device_cadence.rs`; F3 20261026-075325: 698 commands lost in 40 s at 60 before it, live). The creatures' sounds as the console makes them (`play.rs`, F3 pass 25): a creature's sound triggers trip only where `VIHSprite::Process` runs, for an actor `RasterActor` rasters (`World::raster_actors` under the drawn view) and the target, so one behind the camera or a wall plays no swing, hit or step (`Play::keep_rastered_trips`; this client poses every creature within its off distance); and with more than the game's 24 shown (NPC/PC Display) only the 24 nearest the listener make theirs, the PS2's slots (`heard_slots`, `hush`) |

the output watched once a frame (`Watch`): a device gone (its own word, `DeviceNotAvailable`, as WASAPI and Core Audio say it; or no callback in 2 s, as an ALSA device lost does) puts the driver on the wall clock (its frames dropped, the game's sound keeping its clock) while an output is looked for at once, then after 1 s and waits doubling to 30 s, and the first stream that calls back takes the driver back where it is; a start with no output looks the same way; the log says when it stopped, a failure once, and when one plays (F3 crash 20261024-001640's log: a PC woken from sleep, its device gone, the sound lost until a restart); In a page the driver runs on the game's thread, mixed a little ahead of the page's audio clock for an AudioWorklet that only plays what it is handed (`Audio::feed`, `web/sound.js`), and the music, which a page can read only asynchronously, is read whole before the device may start it (`bgm.rs`: `Disc::ready`, asked through `Link::bgm_ready`); natively too, on a thread of its own (`bgm::read_on_a_thread`), so the driver, under the lock the output's callback and the game's thread both take, never waits on the disc (F3 20261029-025532: a callback once mixing for 8,363 ms) | `audio.rs`'s tests: a device gone left for another at once and said back only at the new stream's first callback (a stream calling back every 30 ms for a minute the control), a stream silent 2 s gone and not a frame sooner, a stream that opens but never plays a failed look, the looks' waits (1, then 2, 4, 8, 16, 30 s), each failure said once, the driver on the wall clock while there is no output and waiting with it off; each failing with its planted bug; live (`eqoa boot`, cpal on PulseAudio): the device taken as gone, and its stream paused, each stopped, opened again and played. `bgm.rs`'s test: a BGM streamed through the page's cache, its file handed over frames after it was asked for, equals the disc's stream for 20 seconds sample for sample, never read before it is there (a copy one bit off differs); natively, through the thread with a read 400 ms slow, the same, and no call of the driver's held for half of it (the control: the driver's own slow read holds its call for all of it); the gate leaves `oracle_snd`/`oracle_snddev` equal. The emitter query against brute force over every placed actor (`vi/tests/emitters.rs`: TUNARIA's 1,101 music regions, 4,404 points, keys and order equal; a moved key or the wrong room fails it). The scene logic is otherwise held to the decomp by reading and by its WAVs (footsteps every 400 ms walking, the battle track and back), not yet by an oracle. The weather's intensity damps the ambient track, and the atmosphere's rain (`rain_lt`, two voices) and thunder (`lightning_far02`) play out of SKY.CSF (resident). The tracks are damped by the outdoors probe (`vi::world::Outdoors`). The effects' sound sprites (SPELLFX's: a swing's hits among them) are heard by `spells.rs` as `VIScene::UpdateSoundEmitters` hears them: each sound actor has its own copy of its sprite (`CreateActor` copies a sprite whose `GetInstType` is 1), so its own voice, pan and attenuation, started the first frame its sphere holds the camera and stopped when it no longer does or the actor goes; 32 at most. Measured against eqoa-port on a quad attack: one start a strike, 14 against 14 (the client's earlier one voice a sprite started 3, so a quad attack's strikes after the first were silent). Not ported: groups' battle music |

the batching and culling change no pixel: six `shot`s (a zone, walks in Tunaria and Odus, the river, the human) byte for byte the same as before, `shot` scrolling its layers by the script's time so a shot is the same picture every run; `render/tests/shadow.rs` (a shadow 0.02 under an opaque ground drawn only for its bias; no bias, the other winding, nothing set the controls); Shadows Off byte for byte the picture before the pass (a build of the commit before it); `render/tests/fog16.rs` (fog fans over a frame against the GS's own steps in integers, each pixel under its own DIMX entry: one strong, six middling and twelve faint equal fans exactly at every pixel, the faint red lift never showing and the blue darkening more than at eight bits; mixed fans within 6 over the frame; the sequential reference exactly at every pixel, mixed fans too; the pixels no fan covers untouched and the fans drawn as they are by a renderer not told it can add the sums up, the controls; the dither left out fails it; `-- --ignored` its cost); live on a go-eqoa-server of one's own against eqoa-port's software GS (`VI_GS=cpu`, the GS's arithmetic: what the fog adds over the frame without it in the box it fills, eqoa-port's own offset taken off by a wall beside it), at Freeport's sign post's blue fog by night (0.9, 6.2, 11.3) there, (3.8, 9.6, 14.3) here and (8.9, 17.4, 23.5) as it was, by day (-2.6, 2.6, 8.6), (-1.7, 4.7, 12.7) and (3.6, 12.2, 21.0), and at the white night fog east of it (33.9, 36.5, 33.1), (35.7, 37.4, 34.8) and (57.4, 60.0, 56.0), the sequential reference within 1 to 3 levels of eqoa-port at all three; its cost 0.55 ms more a 1920x1080 frame than the fog as it is on an RX 580 (60 overlapping fans), the page at its rate either way (WebGPU 60 fps, WebGL2 its 30) with no more CPU; and the world's particles of blend mode 3 (the Red Swarming Death's swarm, the rotting creatures' flies and stench, smoke, some spells' dark auras, rain and snow: every particle's and weather's material is `SetDither(0)`'s too) the same way with the options' Particles at PS2 (`Renderer::set_ps2_particles`, `billboard.rs`; Exact, a window's viewport, or a renderer that cannot add the sums up, draws them as they are), their sums from the billboard program's fragments (`billboard.wgsl` `fs_sums`, `fs_spread`) into the same copy and sums (one set for the fans and the particles): many faint puffs stop short of their colour, each its own, where eight bits a channel piled the swarm's fifty-odd on a pixel into a dark mass (`render/tests/particles16.rs`: one strong, six middling, twelve faint and fifty swarm-like equal particles the GS's integers exactly at every pixel, the fifty short of the colour eight bits reach; mixed within 6 over the frame and over half of it; the sequential reference exactly, mixed too; the switch off, no sums and an additive particle drawn as they are and the pixels none covers untouched, the controls; the runs never marked fails it; `-- --ignored` its cost, +0.5 ms a 1920x1080 frame on an RX 580 with a whole swarm in view); live on a go-eqoa-server of one's own beside eqoa-port's software GS at the same moment (its DMA recorded, `VI_DMA_REC`, and replayed through `VI_GS=cpu`; its GPU path rounds each blend where the GS cuts, so its swarm is redder and brighter than a PS2's): the swarm's pixels (39.5, 11.8, 12.3) on the GS, (42.0, 12.0, 12.4) here and (49.5, 13.0, 12.2) at eight bits a channel, and the sequential reference within 3 levels of the sums; a spell's dark aura (SPELLFX's 11th, 0x8E4AA1F7) darker and speckled, as the GS shows a faint darkening twice over, the sums within a level of the reference; Exact the old picture byte for byte (eight spells' shots), and PS2 too where no particle of mode 3 is drawn. The additive particles (the fires, the glows, most spells) are still blended at eight bits, where the GS's cut takes off every lift under five levels |
| `render` | wgpu: instanced models, a texture cache by DictID, the strip programs' lighting (colour + ambient + sun, alpha as sun visibility; the ambient each material's own plus its emissive colour, as `VIRaster::UploadPerMaterial` uploads it, so an emissive material stays lit by night: the Sword of Virtue's blade and some 280 items', the world's crystals and lamps, creatures' eyes, `render/tests/emissive.rs`, against PCSX2 in shots of both clients side by side) and back-face culling, GPU skinning (`VIRStrip4`'s four weighted palette matrices), and linear fog on view depth as `VIRaster::SetFog` sets it, the world's far plane the fog's (`CalcFarPlane`, `fog.rs`; the sky's its own), colour arithmetic on the bytes as the GS does it (no sRGB anywhere), and one pass per material layer with `VIMaterial::ConstructTextPass`'s GS state: the alpha test or one of four blend modes (add, subtract, alpha, multiply), colour-only writes for blended layers, wrap modes, the UV transform and its scroll, the layer colour as c/255; every texture's mip levels as the surface cache keeps them (down to 16 a side, `esf::Surface::decode_levels`) and sampled as TEX1 makes the GS (`VIEESurfCache::Set`: MMIN trilinear, LCM 0, so the level is log2(w) + K, w the depth before the camera, the strip programs' 1/Q, and K the layer's bias as `(char)(int)(bias * -127)` sixteenths, `gs_lod_k`; the game's default 0.8 keeps the base to about 80 units, level 1 to 160, level 2 to 320), the effect volumes', particles', strips' and rings' made-in-code materials at the default bias, all on the base under Extended's Mipmaps Off (`set_mipmaps`; `tests/mipmaps.rs` on the GPU); offscreen capture; a streamed zone uploaded as one group (`upload_group`): one vertex, index, instance and static-light buffer, materials shared across groups by texture, sampler and uniform, an identity palette shared, and its commands recorded into render bundles in runs of 32 models, in order, a run wholly outside the view frustum skipped (wgpu tracks every resource a frame names at every submit, which was 14,000 round Freeport); every model drawn relative to the eye (`View::eye`: the instance's translation less the eye first, the view-projection with the eye at the origin built in doubles), so a world position near Freeport's x 25,300, where a float steps 2 mm, never reaches the GPU: the product had given each model a depth error of centimetres (a helm over a head, a lamp on a table trading places), and Mesa's GLSL (GL, and Firefox's WebGL2 on Linux) multiplies `model * pos` first, which let the obelisks' glow shell, 3 cm behind their stone, come through; a character whose body is a skinned LOD sprite (the player races' models: a near mesh to 10, a far one to 100) draws one level (`GpuModel::level`, `Batch::lod`), as `VILODSprite::Raster` picks it by distance, and none of it past its off distance (`VIScene::RasterActor`; `eqoa/src/lod.rs`), both measured from no farther than the game's camera could be: the follow orbit's eye drawn in along its line to the game's 8.0 (`lod::eye`), so the client's longer zoom only moves the camera back (the player's own body keeps its near level, F3 20261021-001920, and as many characters are drawn round the player as at the console's farthest); the options' Level of Detail off draws every LOD body's near level out to its off distance; both levels at once put the far mesh's triangles through the near one's skin; the passes write colour only, so the target's alpha stays 1 (a page's WebGL2 canvas is composited with it, and a picture taken of it wrapped every alpha-tested edge's colour round); the options' Brightness (the client's: the PS2 left it to the TV) puts the finished scene through a table of 256 levels before the labels, the overlay and the HUD, which keep the game's (`render::curve`: by the stored byte, so the same on every GPU, WebGL2's too; at the middle step no pass at all); the options' Render Scale draws the whole frame, scene, plates, labels and 2D layer, 2 or 4 times the target's size each way into a texture of its own with its own depth buffer and averages each block into a pixel, of the stored bytes, rounded half up (`render::scale`: at 1 no pass; no side past 8192, `scale::fit`, x3 or x2 where x4 does not fit); and the options' HUD Sharp draws the 2D layer's other textures as Text's Sharp draws a font, kept to the quad's own texels, a clear texel mixed by its alpha alone (`ui::HudFilter`, ui.wgsl's `mix_clear`); the radial flora's plants as `VIRStrip6` draws them (`upload_flora`, each sprite a model with room for 700, its runs in the drawing order `Draw::Instances`): no normal or vertex colour, so the ambient plus the sun times 0.15 and the point light's falloff times 0.15 at the distance in the plant's own space, and its fade the vertex alpha (`tests/flora.rs`); immediate-mode fans as `VIRFan` draws them (`render::fans`, the effect volumes'): each fan's front face (its first four vertices: the volumes' fans are both faces of a quad and `VIRFan`'s fixed winding parity culls one), lit as `VIRStrip` lights a vertex (colour + ambient + sun + the point light, clamped), fogged, the texel modulated (alpha the texel's times the vertex's) and blended by the material's mode, Z tested and not written; one instance buffer a frame and a draw call per material run, after the sky's billboards and before the particles, as `VIScene::Render` draws its deferred actors; the ground fog's fans, under the options' Ground Fog at PS2 (the default), blended as the GS blends them into the game's 16-bit frame (`render::fog16`: `VIRaster::Init` makes the frame PSMCT16S and sets DTHE in all four draw environments, and a material without the dither flag, an effect volume's, is drawn under DIMX 0x2031312020313120, entries 0 to 3, so every blend adds the pixel's entry and cuts the low three bits: a fan's lift of four levels or less never shows, a darkening of four or less shows twice over, and the fog stops a good way short of its own colour): a blend cannot read the frame each fan finds, so the fans add up their number, alpha, alpha squared and alpha times colour on each pixel into two float textures, Z tested as they are drawn, then in a second pass alpha times the colour's distance from that mean squared into a third, over a copy of the frame before them, and the GS's own steps run over as many fans, a standard deviation's alpha above the mean and one below and a standard deviation's colour above and below, in turn (the count and every sum kept; alphas leaning so far that the one below would be under 0, as many equal fans as keep the sums of the alphas and of their squares; a fan of As 0, which changes nothing, not counted), from the copy as five bits a channel, under the pixel's own matrix entry (the console's 4 by 4 dither in the fog, as on a PS2), the frame changed by what they change it; equal fans come out as the GS's exactly, every pixel; the reference is the GS a fan at a time (`set_ps2_sequential`, `EQOA_FOG16=sequential`: a copy and three passes a fan), and over Freeport's sign post's fog at night the sums come within 1 to 2 levels of it, where a blend of eight bits a channel is 6 to 9 too bright (equal fans of the sums alone, or the four entries averaged, posterised the fog into blotches the GS does not draw); made only while a fan of the fog is drawn and only over the rectangle its fans cover, and on no frame larger than 3840 by 2160 (`fog16::MAX_PIXELS`: the copy and the sums are 24 bytes a pixel; a larger one, a Render Scale above 4K, draws the fog as it is); where the target cannot be copied (a page's canvas, a GL window) the scene goes through the curve's texture (`Curve::set_through`), and an adapter without float colour buffers to blend (`fog16::supported`) draws the fog as it is; the characters' blob shadows (`render::shadow`: `SetSky`'s material, shadow.tga modulated, blend mode 3, clamped on both axes, Z tested and not written, fogged, back faces culled as `VIRFan` culls them, `SetZBias(15)` as the GS has it, a constant 2490 of the screen Z, which is a shift of 1/w, its pull taken from the game's camera (8 at most) as every other RasterActor distance; drawn after the particle system's billboards and before the weather's); the billboard program's draws (`render::billboard`: the particles, a held item's glow among them, the weather, the stars and the lightning patches, and the spells' bolts and the weapons' trails) the texel modulated by the colour as the GS's MODULATE does (the program sends it × 128) and covering by the texel's alpha times their own, whether the layer tests or blends: the program sends the alpha × 64 when the layer blends (`BeginBillboards`' alpha scale, × 128 when it tests), MODULATE makes As the texel's 0..255 times that over 128, and the blend takes As over 128 (0x80 one), so the 64 is what keeps a texel of 255 from blending twice over, not a half (read as one, every particle, epic weapon's glow and bolt drew at half the game's cover until 2026-10-30); Dynamic Lighting (`lit`, Extended > Lighting, off by default): a second set of model pipelines, shader.wgsl's text with `lit.wgsl`'s entry points after it, that light per fragment what the strip programs light per vertex (the vertex colour, ambient, emissive and static light per vertex; the sun by the interpolated normal made a unit again, times the sun visibility; the view's `lights` with the personal light first, 16 in all, each the programs' (1 − d/r)·N·L̂; clamped to 1; a radial flora plant `VIRStrip6`'s falloff × 0.15), a fixed light (a fire's) left out on a baked instance (`GpuModel::baked`, `GroupPart::baked`, `fs_lit_baked`), the lights in a uniform of their own (group 3) with a mask of them for each of 24 by 16 screen tiles, each light's sphere box projected on the CPU, a fragment taking its tile's alone (exact: a point in reach is inside the sphere, whose projection is inside the box's); a group records lit bundles beside its own while it is on (`relight`), and a view without lights (the loading screen's gears, a window's model) draws the PS2's way; Sun Shadows (`sunshadow`, Extended > Lighting, off by default, drawn only with Dynamic Lighting on): a shadow map of four cascades (a 2D depth array, 2,048 a side natively, 1,024 in a page and on Android), the spheres round the view's depth slices 0 to 19, 19 to 43, 43 to 94 and 94 to 250, each drawn every frame (drawn in turn, as at first, the far two left a moving thing's shadow where it had been at every other frame, and its sunlit side in it), each an orthographic view along the sun as it is that frame, built in doubles relative to the eye and snapped to its texels, which stay put in the world as the camera moves and as the sun turns (`sunshadow::Grid`: each cascade takes back what the turn moves the middle of its slice by, so a point moves within its texels by its depth from there times the turn, and the shadows move with the sun a little every frame; taken in eighths of a degree, as at first, they stood still for 1.25 s and then jumped, and the light's axes turned from the world's origin instead swept the texels through Freeport a dozen a frame), the sun's path's own axis the map's up; drawn each frame the sun is up from the frame's opaque layers whose first layer is opaque, both faces, a vertex whose normal is turned from the sun put back 0.5 along the light (a cave's roof or a ceiling casts; an awning's patches and a roof's planks a little over its top do not shadow it), a layer whose texels' least alpha times its colour's is 0.5 or more as depth alone and any other alpha tested as fs_main tests it (leaves cut out); a zone's casters recorded at its upload in clusters of members by the 48-unit cell their box's middle is in (the terrain and the sprites placed all over the zone apart), each culled against a cascade's box (an ordinary cluster only as far toward the sun as a caster 150 over what it shadows could reach from), and the models by the world box their instances were last set to; the seams not blended: an edge moves by under a pixel as one passes it, and blending cost 0.16 ms at 1080p) and a plant's sun the map's, a module of their own (the lit pipelines alone untouched), and the groups' runs through bundles recorded for them;
